\documentclass[11pt]{article}

    \usepackage[breakable]{tcolorbox}
    \usepackage{parskip} % Stop auto-indenting (to mimic markdown behaviour)
    

    % Basic figure setup, for now with no caption control since it's done
    % automatically by Pandoc (which extracts ![](path) syntax from Markdown).
    \usepackage{graphicx}
    % Keep aspect ratio if custom image width or height is specified
    \setkeys{Gin}{keepaspectratio}
    % Maintain compatibility with old templates. Remove in nbconvert 6.0
    \let\Oldincludegraphics\includegraphics
    % Ensure that by default, figures have no caption (until we provide a
    % proper Figure object with a Caption API and a way to capture that
    % in the conversion process - todo).
    \usepackage{caption}
    \DeclareCaptionFormat{nocaption}{}
    \captionsetup{format=nocaption,aboveskip=0pt,belowskip=0pt}

    \usepackage{float}
    \floatplacement{figure}{H} % forces figures to be placed at the correct location
    \usepackage{xcolor} % Allow colors to be defined
    \usepackage{enumerate} % Needed for markdown enumerations to work
    \usepackage{geometry} % Used to adjust the document margins
    \usepackage{amsmath} % Equations
    \usepackage{amssymb} % Equations
    \usepackage{textcomp} % defines textquotesingle
    % Hack from http://tex.stackexchange.com/a/47451/13684:
    \AtBeginDocument{%
        \def\PYZsq{\textquotesingle}% Upright quotes in Pygmentized code
    }
    \usepackage{upquote} % Upright quotes for verbatim code
    \usepackage{eurosym} % defines \euro

    \usepackage{iftex}
    \ifPDFTeX
        \usepackage[T1]{fontenc}
        \IfFileExists{alphabeta.sty}{
              \usepackage{alphabeta}
          }{
              \usepackage[mathletters]{ucs}
              \usepackage[utf8x]{inputenc}
          }
    \else
        \usepackage{fontspec}
        \usepackage{unicode-math}
    \fi

    \usepackage{fancyvrb} % verbatim replacement that allows latex
    \usepackage{grffile} % extends the file name processing of package graphics
                         % to support a larger range
    \makeatletter % fix for old versions of grffile with XeLaTeX
    \@ifpackagelater{grffile}{2019/11/01}
    {
      % Do nothing on new versions
    }
    {
      \def\Gread@@xetex#1{%
        \IfFileExists{"\Gin@base".bb}%
        {\Gread@eps{\Gin@base.bb}}%
        {\Gread@@xetex@aux#1}%
      }
    }
    \makeatother
    \usepackage[Export]{adjustbox} % Used to constrain images to a maximum size
    \adjustboxset{max size={0.9\linewidth}{0.9\paperheight}}

    % The hyperref package gives us a pdf with properly built
    % internal navigation ('pdf bookmarks' for the table of contents,
    % internal cross-reference links, web links for URLs, etc.)
    \usepackage{hyperref}
    % The default LaTeX title has an obnoxious amount of whitespace. By default,
    % titling removes some of it. It also provides customization options.
    \usepackage{titling}
    \usepackage{longtable} % longtable support required by pandoc >1.10
    \usepackage{booktabs}  % table support for pandoc > 1.12.2
    \usepackage{array}     % table support for pandoc >= 2.11.3
    \usepackage{calc}      % table minipage width calculation for pandoc >= 2.11.1
    \usepackage[inline]{enumitem} % IRkernel/repr support (it uses the enumerate* environment)
    \usepackage[normalem]{ulem} % ulem is needed to support strikethroughs (\sout)
                                % normalem makes italics be italics, not underlines
    \usepackage{soul}      % strikethrough (\st) support for pandoc >= 3.0.0
    \usepackage{mathrsfs}
    

    
    % Colors for the hyperref package
    \definecolor{urlcolor}{rgb}{0,.145,.698}
    \definecolor{linkcolor}{rgb}{.71,0.21,0.01}
    \definecolor{citecolor}{rgb}{.12,.54,.11}

    % ANSI colors
    \definecolor{ansi-black}{HTML}{3E424D}
    \definecolor{ansi-black-intense}{HTML}{282C36}

    % commands and environments needed by pandoc snippets

    \newcommand{\DataTypeTok}[1]{\textcolor[rgb]{0.56,0.13,0.00}{{#1}}}
    \newcommand{\DecValTok}[1]{\textcolor[rgb]{0.25,0.63,0.44}{{#1}}}
    \newcommand{\BaseNTok}[1]{\textcolor[rgb]{0.25,0.63,0.44}{{#1}}}
    \newcommand{\FloatTok}[1]{\textcolor[rgb]{0.25,0.63,0.44}{{#1}}}
    \newcommand{\CharTok}[1]{\textcolor[rgb]{0.25,0.44,0.63}{{#1}}}
    \newcommand{\StringTok}[1]{\textcolor[rgb]{0.25,0.44,0.63}{{#1}}}
    \newcommand{\CommentTok}[1]{\textcolor[rgb]{0.38,0.63,0.69}{\textit{{#1}}}}
    \newcommand{\OtherTok}[1]{\textcolor[rgb]{0.00,0.44,0.13}{{#1}}}
    \newcommand{\AlertTok}[1]{\textcolor[rgb]{1.00,0.00,0.00}{\textbf{{#1}}}}
    \newcommand{\FunctionTok}[1]{\textcolor[rgb]{0.02,0.16,0.49}{{#1}}}
    \newcommand{\RegionMarkerTok}[1]{{#1}}

    % Additional commands for more recent versions of Pandoc
    \newcommand{\ConstantTok}[1]{\textcolor[rgb]{0.53,0.00,0.00}{{#1}}}
    \newcommand{\SpecialCharTok}[1]{\textcolor[rgb]{0.25,0.44,0.63}{{#1}}}
    \newcommand{\VerbatimStringTok}[1]{\textcolor[rgb]{0.25,0.44,0.63}{{#1}}}
    \newcommand{\SpecialStringTok}[1]{\textcolor[rgb]{0.73,0.40,0.53}{{#1}}}
    \newcommand{\ImportTok}[1]{{#1}}
    \newcommand{\DocumentationTok}[1]{\textcolor[rgb]{0.73,0.13,0.13}{\textit{{#1}}}}
    \newcommand{\AnnotationTok}[1]{\textcolor[rgb]{0.38,0.63,0.69}{\textbf{\textit{{#1}}}}}
    \newcommand{\CommentVarTok}[1]{\textcolor[rgb]{0.38,0.63,0.69}{\textbf{\textit{{#1}}}}}
    \newcommand{\VariableTok}[1]{\textcolor[rgb]{0.10,0.09,0.49}{{#1}}}
    \newcommand{\ControlFlowTok}[1]{\textcolor[rgb]{0.00,0.44,0.13}{\textbf{{#1}}}}
    \newcommand{\OperatorTok}[1]{\textcolor[rgb]{0.40,0.40,0.40}{{#1}}}
    \newcommand{\BuiltInTok}[1]{{#1}}
    \newcommand{\ExtensionTok}[1]{{#1}}
    \newcommand{\PreprocessorTok}[1]{\textcolor[rgb]{0.74,0.48,0.00}{{#1}}}
    \newcommand{\AttributeTok}[1]{\textcolor[rgb]{0.49,0.56,0.16}{{#1}}}
    \newcommand{\InformationTok}[1]{\textcolor[rgb]{0.38,0.63,0.69}{\textbf{\textit{{#1}}}}}
    \newcommand{\WarningTok}[1]{\textcolor[rgb]{0.38,0.63,0.69}{\textbf{\textit{{#1}}}}}
    \makeatletter
    \newsavebox\pandoc@box
    \newcommand*\pandocbounded[1]{%
      \sbox\pandoc@box{#1}%
      % scaling factors for width and height
      \Gscale@div\@tempa\textheight{\dimexpr\ht\pandoc@box+\dp\pandoc@box\relax}%
      \Gscale@div\@tempb\linewidth{\wd\pandoc@box}%
      % select the smaller of both
      \ifdim\@tempb\p@<\@tempa\p@
        \let\@tempa\@tempb
      \fi
      % scaling accordingly (\@tempa < 1)
      \ifdim\@tempa\p@<\p@
        \scalebox{\@tempa}{\usebox\pandoc@box}%
      % scaling not needed, use as it is
      \else
        \usebox{\pandoc@box}%
      \fi
    }
    \makeatother

    % Define a nice break command that doesn't care if a line doesn't already
    % exist.
    \def\br{\hspace*{\fill} \\* }
    % Math Jax compatibility definitions
    \def\gt{>}
    \def\lt{<}
    \let\Oldtex\TeX
    \let\Oldlatex\LaTeX
    \renewcommand{\TeX}{\textrm{\Oldtex}}
    \renewcommand{\LaTeX}{\textrm{\Oldlatex}}

% Pygments definitions
\makeatletter
\def\PY@reset{\let\PY@it=\relax \let\PY@bf=\relax%
    \let\PY@ul=\relax \let\PY@tc=\relax%
    \let\PY@bc=\relax \let\PY@ff=\relax}
\def\PY@tok#1{\csname PY@tok@#1\endcsname}
\def\PY@toks#1+{\ifx\relax#1\empty\else%
    \PY@tok{#1}\expandafter\PY@toks\fi}
\def\PY@do#1{\PY@bc{\PY@tc{\PY@ul{%
    \PY@it{\PY@bf{\PY@ff{#1}}}}}}}
\def\PY#1#2{\PY@reset\PY@toks#1+\relax+\PY@do{#2}}

\def\PYZbs{\char`\\}

\def\PYZob{\char`\{}

\def\PYZca{\char`\^}

\def\PYZlt{\char`\<}
\def\PYZgt{\char`\>}
\def\PYZsh{\char`\#}
\def\PYZpc{\char`\%}
\def\PYZdl{\char`\$}

    % For linebreaks inside Verbatim environment from package fancyvrb.
    \makeatletter
        \newbox\Wrappedcontinuationbox
        \newbox\Wrappedvisiblespacebox
        \newcommand*\Wrappedvisiblespace {\textcolor{red}{\textvisiblespace}}
        \newcommand*\Wrappedcontinuationsymbol {\textcolor{red}{\llap{\tiny$\m@th\hookrightarrow$}}}
        \newcommand*\Wrappedcontinuationindent {3ex }
        \newcommand*\Wrappedafterbreak {\kern\Wrappedcontinuationindent\copy\Wrappedcontinuationbox}
        % Take advantage of the already applied Pygments mark-up to insert
        % potential linebreaks for TeX processing.
        %        {, <, #, %, $, ' and ": go to next line.
        %        _, }, ^, &, >, - and ~: stay at end of broken line.
        % Use of \textquotesingle for straight quote.
        \newcommand*\Wrappedbreaksatspecials {%
            \def\PYGZus{\discretionary{\char`\_}{\Wrappedafterbreak}{\char`\_}}%
            \def\PYGZob{\discretionary{}{\Wrappedafterbreak\char`\{}{\char`\{}}%
            \def\PYGZcb{\discretionary{\char`\}}{\Wrappedafterbreak}{\char`\}}}%
            \def\PYGZca{\discretionary{\char`\^}{\Wrappedafterbreak}{\char`\^}}%
            \def\PYGZam{\discretionary{\char`\&}{\Wrappedafterbreak}{\char`\&}}%
            \def\PYGZlt{\discretionary{}{\Wrappedafterbreak\char`\<}{\char`\<}}%
            \def\PYGZgt{\discretionary{\char`\>}{\Wrappedafterbreak}{\char`\>}}%
            \def\PYGZsh{\discretionary{}{\Wrappedafterbreak\char`\#}{\char`\#}}%
            \def\PYGZpc{\discretionary{}{\Wrappedafterbreak\char`\%}{\char`\%}}%
            \def\PYGZdl{\discretionary{}{\Wrappedafterbreak\char`\$}{\char`\$}}%
            \def\PYGZhy{\discretionary{\char`\-}{\Wrappedafterbreak}{\char`\-}}%
            \def\PYGZsq{\discretionary{}{\Wrappedafterbreak\textquotesingle}{\textquotesingle}}%
            \def\PYGZdq{\discretionary{}{\Wrappedafterbreak\char`\"}{\char`\"}}%
            \def\PYGZti{\discretionary{\char`\~}{\Wrappedafterbreak}{\char`\~}}%
        }
        % Some characters . , ; ? ! / are not pygmentized.
        % This macro makes them "active" and they will insert potential linebreaks
        \newcommand*\Wrappedbreaksatpunct {%
            \lccode`\~`\.\lowercase{\def~}{\discretionary{\hbox{\char`\.}}{\Wrappedafterbreak}{\hbox{\char`\.}}}%
            \lccode`\~`\,\lowercase{\def~}{\discretionary{\hbox{\char`\,}}{\Wrappedafterbreak}{\hbox{\char`\,}}}%
            \lccode`\~`\;\lowercase{\def~}{\discretionary{\hbox{\char`\;}}{\Wrappedafterbreak}{\hbox{\char`\;}}}%
            \lccode`\~`\:\lowercase{\def~}{\discretionary{\hbox{\char`\:}}{\Wrappedafterbreak}{\hbox{\char`\:}}}%
            \lccode`\~`\?\lowercase{\def~}{\discretionary{\hbox{\char`\?}}{\Wrappedafterbreak}{\hbox{\char`\?}}}%
            \lccode`\~`\!\lowercase{\def~}{\discretionary{\hbox{\char`\!}}{\Wrappedafterbreak}{\hbox{\char`\!}}}%
            \lccode`\~`\/\lowercase{\def~}{\discretionary{\hbox{\char`\/}}{\Wrappedafterbreak}{\hbox{\char`\/}}}%
            \catcode`\.\active
            \catcode`\,\active
            \catcode`\;\active
            \catcode`\:\active
            \catcode`\?\active
            \catcode`\!\active
            \catcode`\/\active
            \lccode`\~`\~
        }
    \makeatother

    \let\OriginalVerbatim=\Verbatim
    \makeatletter
    \renewcommand{\Verbatim}[1][1]{%
        %\parskip\z@skip
        \sbox\Wrappedcontinuationbox {\Wrappedcontinuationsymbol}%
        \sbox\Wrappedvisiblespacebox {\FV@SetupFont\Wrappedvisiblespace}%
        \def\FancyVerbFormatLine ##1{\hsize\linewidth
            \vtop{\raggedright\hyphenpenalty\z@\exhyphenpenalty\z@
                \doublehyphendemerits\z@\finalhyphendemerits\z@
                \strut ##1\strut}%
        }%
        % If the linebreak is at a space, the latter will be displayed as visible
        % space at end of first line, and a continuation symbol starts next line.
        % Stretch/shrink are however usually zero for typewriter font.
        \def\FV@Space {%
            \nobreak\hskip\z@ plus\fontdimen3\font minus\fontdimen4\font
            \discretionary{\copy\Wrappedvisiblespacebox}{\Wrappedafterbreak}
            {\kern\fontdimen2\font}%
        }%

        % Allow breaks at special characters using \PYG... macros.
        \Wrappedbreaksatspecials
        % Breaks at punctuation characters . , ; ? ! and / need catcode=\active
        \OriginalVerbatim[#1,codes*=\Wrappedbreaksatpunct]%
    }
    \makeatother

    % Exact colors from NB
    \definecolor{incolor}{HTML}{303F9F}
    \definecolor{outcolor}{HTML}{D84315}

    % prompt
    \makeatletter
    \newcommand{\boxspacing}{\kern\kvtcb@left@rule\kern\kvtcb@boxsep}
    \makeatother
    \newcommand{\prompt}[4]{
        {\ttfamily\llap{{\color{#2}[#3]:\hspace{3pt}#4}}\vspace{-\baselineskip}}
    }
    

    
    % Prevent overflowing lines due to hard-to-break entities
    \sloppy
    % Setup hyperref package
    \hypersetup{
      breaklinks=true,  % so long urls are correctly broken across lines
      colorlinks=true,
      urlcolor=urlcolor,
      linkcolor=linkcolor,

    \section{🛰️ Semantic Segmentation with Deep
Learning}\label{semantic-segmentation-with-deep-learning}

\subsection{Complete Detailed Tutorial --- Potsdam
Dataset}\label{complete-detailed-tutorial-potsdam-dataset}

\begin{center}\rule{0.5\linewidth}{0.5pt}\end{center}

\textbf{Who is this for?} This notebook is written for someone who: -
Knows basic Python (loops, functions, lists) - Wants to learn
\textbf{computer vision AI} in practice - Wants to \textbf{understand,
see, and execute} at the same time

\textbf{By the end of this notebook you will understand:} - How
satellite/aerial datasets work - How a neural network labels an image
pixel-by-pixel - What a simple CNN architecture is and how it works -
What U-Net is and why it outperforms a plain CNN

\begin{center}\rule{0.5\linewidth}{0.5pt}\end{center}

\begin{quote}
\textbf{💡 How to use:} Read each Markdown cell, then run the code cell
below it (▶ button). Never skip a step --- each cell builds on the
previous one!
\end{quote}

    \begin{center}\rule{0.5\linewidth}{0.5pt}\end{center}

\subsection{📚 Concept 1: What is Semantic
Segmentation?}\label{concept-1-what-is-semantic-segmentation}

\subsubsection{The story starts
here\ldots{}}\label{the-story-starts-here}

Imagine a drone took an aerial photo of a city. We want to know:
\textbf{What does every pixel of this image belong to?} A building? A
tree? A car? A road?

This task is called \textbf{Semantic Segmentation}.

\subsubsection{Difference from Object
Detection:}\label{difference-from-object-detection}

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  \textbf{Object Detection}: draws a bounding box around an object →
  ``there is a car here''
\item
  \textbf{Semantic Segmentation}: for \textbf{every pixel} says → ``this
  pixel is a car, that pixel is a tree''
\end{itemize}

\begin{verbatim}
Input: Aerial image              Output: Color-coded map
┌─────────────────┐               ┌─────────────────┐
│  (RGB image)    │  → AI model→  │  Yellow = Car   │
│                 │               │  Green  = Tree  │
│                 │               │  Blue   = Bldg  │
└─────────────────┘               └─────────────────┘
\end{verbatim}

\subsubsection{Why does it matter?}\label{why-does-it-matter}

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  🚗 Self-driving cars (each pixel → road/sidewalk/car)
\item
  🏥 Medical imaging (each pixel → tumor/healthy tissue)
\item
  🛰️ Urban mapping (buildings, roads, green areas)
\end{itemize}

    \begin{center}\rule{0.5\linewidth}{0.5pt}\end{center}

\subsection{📦 Concept 2: The Potsdam
Dataset}\label{concept-2-the-potsdam-dataset}

\subsubsection{Why Potsdam?}\label{why-potsdam}

\textbf{Potsdam} is a city in Germany. The ISPRS organization captured
aerial images at \textbf{5 cm resolution} (1 pixel = 5×5 cm on the
ground) and \textbf{manually labeled every single pixel}. This took
years of work and produced an extremely valuable benchmark dataset.

\subsubsection{GeoTIFF Format}\label{geotiff-format}

GeoTIFF is a \textbf{multi-band image} format. Like a colour photo with
3 layers (RGB), but with \textbf{6 layers (bands)}:

\begin{verbatim}
┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│  Band 0: Red                → standard red channel  │
│  Band 1: Green              → standard green channel │
│  Band 2: Blue               → standard blue channel  │
│  Band 3: Infrared (IR)      → invisible to human eye │
│  Band 4: Elevation (DSM)    → height above sea level │
│  Band 5: Labels             → ← THIS is our target! │
└─────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
\end{verbatim}

\subsubsection{6 Label Classes:}\label{label-classes}

\begin{longtable}[]{@{}lll@{}}
\toprule\noalign{}
ID & Class Name & Display Color \\
\midrule\noalign{}
\endhead
\bottomrule\noalign{}
\endlastfoot
0 & Impervious surface (asphalt, pavement) & ⬜ White \\
1 & Building & 🟦 Blue \\
2 & Tree & 🟩 Green \\
3 & Low vegetation (grass, shrubs) & 🩵 Cyan \\
4 & Car & 🟨 Yellow \\
5 & Clutter / Background & 🟥 Red \\
\end{longtable}

\begin{quote}
\textbf{Why Infrared?} Plants strongly reflect infrared light. This band
makes it much easier to separate trees from asphalt.
\end{quote}

    \begin{center}\rule{0.5\linewidth}{0.5pt}\end{center}

\subsection{📦 Step 0 --- Install
Libraries}\label{step-0-install-libraries}

\subsubsection{What does each library
do?}\label{what-does-each-library-do}

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  \textbf{\texttt{rasterio}}: reads GeoTIFF files (like PIL, but for
  geospatial multi-band images)
\item
  \textbf{\texttt{numpy}}: multidimensional numerical arrays (every
  image = a numpy array)
\item
  \textbf{\texttt{matplotlib}}: plotting and image display
\item
  \textbf{\texttt{scikit-learn}}: ML utilities including KFold splitter
\item
  \textbf{\texttt{tensorflow}}: the deep learning framework --- building
  and training neural networks
\end{itemize}

\begin{quote}
⏳ This may take a couple of minutes. \texttt{Successfully\ installed} →
you're good.
\end{quote}

\subsubsection{What is SEED?}\label{what-is-seed}

\texttt{SEED\ =\ 42} makes all random operations reproducible. Same seed
→ same results every run. The number 42 is just a convention!

\subsubsection{What is DATA\_DIR?}\label{what-is-data_dir}

The folder path where the \texttt{.tif} tile files live. ⚠️ If you
downloaded the full dataset elsewhere, change this path.

    \PY{k}{for} \PY{n}{base} \PY{o+ow}{in} \PY{n}{search\PYZus{}bases}\PY{p}{:}

        \PY{n}{candidates}\PY{o}{.}\PY{n}{append}\PY{p}{(}\PY{n}{os}\PY{o}{.}\PY{n}{path}\PY{o}{.}\PY{n}{join}\PY{p}{(}\PY{n}{base}\PY{p}{,} \PY{l+s+s1}{\PYZsq{}}\PY{l+s+s1}{Potsdam\PYZhy{}GeoTif}\PY{l+s+s1}{\PYZsq{}}\PY{p}{)}\PY{p}{)}
        \PY{n}{candidates}\PY{o}{.}\PY{n}{append}\PY{p}{(}\PY{n}{os}\PY{o}{.}\PY{n}{path}\PY{o}{.}\PY{n}{join}\PY{p}{(}\PY{n}{base}\PY{p}{,} \PY{l+s+s1}{\PYZsq{}}\PY{l+s+s1}{data}\PY{l+s+s1}{\PYZsq{}}\PY{p}{)}\PY{p}{)}
    \PY{k}{for} \PY{n}{p} \PY{o+ow}{in} \PY{n}{candidates}\PY{p}{:}
        \PY{k}{if} \PY{n}{os}\PY{o}{.}\PY{n}{path}\PY{o}{.}\PY{n}{isdir}\PY{p}{(}\PY{n}{p}\PY{p}{)}\PY{p}{:}
            \PY{k}{try}\PY{p}{:}
                \PY{k}{if} \PY{n+nb}{any}\PY{p}{(}\PY{n}{f}\PY{o}{.}\PY{n}{endswith}\PY{p}{(}\PY{l+s+s1}{\PYZsq{}}\PY{l+s+s1}{.tif}\PY{l+s+s1}{\PYZsq{}}\PY{p}{)} \PY{k}{for} \PY{n}{f} \PY{o+ow}{in} \PY{n}{os}\PY{o}{.}\PY{n}{listdir}\PY{p}{(}\PY{n}{p}\PY{p}{)}\PY{p}{)}\PY{p}{:}
                    \PY{k}{return} \PY{n}{p}
            \PY{k}{except}\PY{p}{:}
                \PY{k}{continue}
    \PY{n}{search\PYZus{}root} \PY{o}{=} \PY{n}{os}\PY{o}{.}\PY{n}{path}\PY{o}{.}\PY{n}{dirname}\PY{p}{(}\PY{n}{os}\PY{o}{.}\PY{n}{path}\PY{o}{.}\PY{n}{dirname}\PY{p}{(}\PY{n}{cwd}\PY{p}{)}\PY{p}{)}
    \PY{k}{for} \PY{n}{root}\PY{p}{,} \PY{n}{\PYZus{}}\PY{p}{,} \PY{n}{files} \PY{o+ow}{in} \PY{n}{os}\PY{o}{.}\PY{n}{walk}\PY{p}{(}\PY{n}{search\PYZus{}root}\PY{p}{)}\PY{p}{:}
        \PY{k}{if} \PY{n+nb}{any}\PY{p}{(}\PY{n}{f}\PY{o}{.}\PY{n}{endswith}\PY{p}{(}\PY{l+s+s1}{\PYZsq{}}\PY{l+s+s1}{.tif}\PY{l+s+s1}{\PYZsq{}}\PY{p}{)} \PY{k}{for} \PY{n}{f} \PY{o+ow}{in} \PY{n}{files}\PY{p}{)}\PY{p}{:}
            \PY{k}{return} \PY{n}{root}
    \PY{k}{return} \PY{l+s+s1}{\PYZsq{}}\PY{l+s+s1}{data}\PY{l+s+s1}{\PYZsq{}}

\PY{n}{DATA\PYZus{}DIR} \PY{o}{=} \PY{n}{discover\PYZus{}data\PYZus{}dir}\PY{p}{(}\PY{p}{)}
\PY{n}{OUTPUT\PYZus{}DIR} \PY{o}{=} \PY{l+s+s1}{\PYZsq{}}\PY{l+s+s1}{/kaggle/working}\PY{l+s+s1}{\PYZsq{}} \PY{k}{if} \PY{n}{os}\PY{o}{.}\PY{n}{path}\PY{o}{.}\PY{n}{exists}\PY{p}{(}\PY{l+s+s1}{\PYZsq{}}\PY{l+s+s1}{/kaggle/working}\PY{l+s+s1}{\PYZsq{}}\PY{p}{)} \PY{k}{else} \PY{l+s+s1}{\PYZsq{}}\PY{l+s+s1}{.}\PY{l+s+s1}{\PYZsq{}}
\PY{n+nb}{print}\PY{p}{(}\PY{l+s+sa}{f}\PY{l+s+s1}{\PYZsq{}}\PY{l+s+s1}{Data directory: }\PY{l+s+si}{\PYZob{}}\PY{n}{DATA\PYZus{}DIR}\PY{l+s+si}{\PYZcb{}}\PY{l+s+s1}{\PYZsq{}}\PY{p}{)}
\PY{n+nb}{print}\PY{p}{(}\PY{l+s+sa}{f}\PY{l+s+s1}{\PYZsq{}}\PY{l+s+s1}{Output directory for logs and models: }\PY{l+s+si}{\PYZob{}}\PY{n}{OUTPUT\PYZus{}DIR}\PY{l+s+si}{\PYZcb{}}\PY{l+s+s1}{\PYZsq{}}\PY{p}{)}
\end{Verbatim}
\end{tcolorbox}

Output directory for logs and models: /kaggle/working

\section{━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━}\label{section}

\section{Step 1: Dataset Preparation}\label{step-1-dataset-preparation}

\section{━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━}\label{section-1}

\subsection{Goal of this step:}\label{goal-of-this-step}

\begin{enumerate}
\def\labelenumi{\arabic{enumi}.}
\tightlist
\item
  Find all tile files in the dataset directory
\item
  Read one tile and visualise its different bands
\item
  Split the files into 5 groups (folds) for fair evaluation
\end{enumerate}

\subsection{Why is this step
important?}\label{why-is-this-step-important}

Before building any AI model, we must \textbf{understand our data}. Deep
learning without data understanding is like cooking without looking at
the ingredients!

    \subsubsection{1.1 --- Finding GeoTIFF
Files}\label{finding-geotiff-files}

\paragraph{\texorpdfstring{What does \texttt{get\_all\_tif\_files}
do?}{What does get\_all\_tif\_files do?}}\label{what-does-get_all_tif_files-do}

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\NormalTok{os.walk(data\_dir)}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

Recursively visits every folder and sub-folder --- like opening every
directory one by one and listing its files.

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\ControlFlowTok{if}\NormalTok{ f.endswith(}\StringTok{\textquotesingle{}.tif\textquotesingle{}}\NormalTok{)}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

Selects only files whose extension is \texttt{.tif}.

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\NormalTok{tif\_files.append(os.path.join(root, f))}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

Builds the \textbf{full path} (folder + filename) and appends it to our
list.

\begin{quote}
With the full Potsdam dataset: expect 15,000+ files. Right now we work
with one sample tile --- results are illustrative.
\end{quote}

    \begin{tcolorbox}[breakable, size=fbox, boxrule=1pt, pad at break*=1mm,colback=cellbackground, colframe=cellborder]
\prompt{In}{incolor}{8}{\boxspacing}
\begin{Verbatim}[commandchars=\\\{\}]
\PY{k}{def}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n+nf}{get\PYZus{}all\PYZus{}tif\PYZus{}files}\PY{p}{(}\PY{n}{data\PYZus{}dir}\PY{p}{)}\PY{p}{:}
\PY{+w}{    }\PY{l+s+sd}{\PYZdq{}\PYZdq{}\PYZdq{}}
\PY{l+s+sd}{    Recursively finds all .tif files inside data\PYZus{}dir.}

\PY{l+s+sd}{    Args:}
\PY{l+s+sd}{        data\PYZus{}dir (str): root folder to search}
\PY{l+s+sd}{    Returns:}
\PY{l+s+sd}{        list[str]: full paths to every .tif file found}
\PY{l+s+sd}{    \PYZdq{}\PYZdq{}\PYZdq{}}
    \PY{n}{tif\PYZus{}files} \PY{o}{=} \PY{p}{[}\PY{p}{]}  \PY{c+c1}{\PYZsh{} start with an empty list}

    \PY{c+c1}{\PYZsh{} os.walk yields (current\PYZus{}folder, sub\PYZhy{}folders, files)}
    \PY{k}{for} \PY{n}{root}\PY{p}{,} \PY{n}{dirs}\PY{p}{,} \PY{n}{files} \PY{o+ow}{in} \PY{n}{os}\PY{o}{.}\PY{n}{walk}\PY{p}{(}\PY{n}{data\PYZus{}dir}\PY{p}{)}\PY{p}{:}
        \PY{k}{for} \PY{n}{f} \PY{o+ow}{in} \PY{n}{files}\PY{p}{:}
            \PY{k}{if} \PY{n}{f}\PY{o}{.}\PY{n}{endswith}\PY{p}{(}\PY{l+s+s1}{\PYZsq{}}\PY{l+s+s1}{.tif}\PY{l+s+s1}{\PYZsq{}}\PY{p}{)}\PY{p}{:}               \PY{c+c1}{\PYZsh{} only GeoTIFF files}
                \PY{n}{full\PYZus{}path} \PY{o}{=} \PY{n}{os}\PY{o}{.}\PY{n}{path}\PY{o}{.}\PY{n}{join}\PY{p}{(}\PY{n}{root}\PY{p}{,} \PY{n}{f}\PY{p}{)}  \PY{c+c1}{\PYZsh{} build full path}
                \PY{n}{tif\PYZus{}files}\PY{o}{.}\PY{n}{append}\PY{p}{(}\PY{n}{full\PYZus{}path}\PY{p}{)}

    \PY{k}{return} \PY{n}{tif\PYZus{}files}

\PY{n}{all\PYZus{}files} \PY{o}{=} \PY{n}{get\PYZus{}all\PYZus{}tif\PYZus{}files}\PY{p}{(}\PY{n}{DATA\PYZus{}DIR}\PY{p}{)}
\PY{n}{all\PYZus{}files} \PY{o}{=} \PY{n}{all\PYZus{}files}\PY{p}{[}\PY{p}{:}\PY{l+m+mi}{15000}\PY{p}{]}  \PY{c+c1}{\PYZsh{} Kaggle performance limit}

\PY{n+nb}{print}\PY{p}{(}\PY{l+s+sa}{f}\PY{l+s+s1}{\PYZsq{}}\PY{l+s+s1}{GeoTIFF files found: }\PY{l+s+si}{\PYZob{}}\PY{n+nb}{len}\PY{p}{(}\PY{n}{all\PYZus{}files}\PY{p}{)}\PY{l+s+si}{\PYZcb{}}\PY{l+s+s1}{\PYZsq{}}\PY{p}{)}
\PY{n+nb}{print}\PY{p}{(}\PY{l+s+s1}{\PYZsq{}}\PY{l+s+s1}{Files:}\PY{l+s+s1}{\PYZsq{}}\PY{p}{)}
\PY{k}{for} \PY{n}{f} \PY{o+ow}{in} \PY{n}{all\PYZus{}files}\PY{p}{[}\PY{p}{:}\PY{l+m+mi}{10}\PY{p}{]}\PY{p}{:}          \PY{c+c1}{\PYZsh{} show at most 10}
    \PY{n+nb}{print}\PY{p}{(}\PY{l+s+s1}{\PYZsq{}}\PY{l+s+s1}{  •}\PY{l+s+s1}{\PYZsq{}}\PY{p}{,} \PY{n}{os}\PY{o}{.}\PY{n}{path}\PY{o}{.}\PY{n}{basename}\PY{p}{(}\PY{n}{f}\PY{p}{)}\PY{p}{)}
\end{Verbatim}
\end{tcolorbox}

    \begin{Verbatim}[commandchars=\\\{\}]

Files:

  • 0000024416-0000021952.tif

    \subsubsection{1.2 --- Reading and Understanding a
Tile}\label{reading-and-understanding-a-tile}

\paragraph{\texorpdfstring{\texttt{rasterio.open}}{rasterio.open}}\label{rasterio.open}

\texttt{rasterio} is like \texttt{PIL.Image.open} but for geospatial
multi-band images.

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\ControlFlowTok{with}\NormalTok{ rasterio.}\BuiltInTok{open}\NormalTok{(file\_path) }\ImportTok{as}\NormalTok{ src:}
\NormalTok{    data }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ src.read()  }\CommentTok{\# reads ALL bands → numpy array of shape (6, H, W)}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

The data shape \texttt{(6,\ 224,\ 224)} means: - \textbf{6}: number of
bands - \textbf{224}: image height in pixels - \textbf{224}: image width
in pixels

\paragraph{\texorpdfstring{Why
\texttt{normalize\_band}?}{Why normalize\_band?}}\label{why-normalize_band}

Elevation values might range from 100 to 500 metres. \texttt{matplotlib}
expects float images in the range \textbf{0 -- 1}. Normalisation maps
any range to {[}0, 1{]}:

\begin{verbatim}
normalised = (value − min) / (max − min)
Example: (300 − 100) / (500 − 100) = 0.5
\end{verbatim}

\paragraph{\texorpdfstring{Why
\texttt{label\_to\_rgb}?}{Why label\_to\_rgb?}}\label{why-label_to_rgb}

The label band contains integers 0--5 (one per class). For display, we
map each integer to a colour: - 0 → white, 1 → blue, 2 → green, \ldots{}

    \begin{tcolorbox}[breakable, size=fbox, boxrule=1pt, pad at break*=1mm,colback=cellbackground, colframe=cellborder]
\prompt{In}{incolor}{9}{\boxspacing}
\begin{Verbatim}[commandchars=\\\{\}]
\PY{c+c1}{\PYZsh{} ── Helper functions ────────────────────────────────────────────────}

\PY{k}{def}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n+nf}{normalize\PYZus{}band}\PY{p}{(}\PY{n}{band}\PY{p}{)}\PY{p}{:}
\PY{+w}{    }\PY{l+s+sd}{\PYZdq{}\PYZdq{}\PYZdq{}}
\PY{l+s+sd}{    Normalises a 2D array to the range [0.0, 1.0].}

\PY{l+s+sd}{    Formula: normalised = (x \PYZhy{} min) / (max \PYZhy{} min)}

\PY{l+s+sd}{    Why: matplotlib float images must be in [0, 1].}
\PY{l+s+sd}{    Edge case: if all values are identical, return zeros (avoid /0).}
\PY{l+s+sd}{    \PYZdq{}\PYZdq{}\PYZdq{}}
    \PY{n}{b\PYZus{}min}\PY{p}{,} \PY{n}{b\PYZus{}max} \PY{o}{=} \PY{n}{band}\PY{o}{.}\PY{n}{min}\PY{p}{(}\PY{p}{)}\PY{p}{,} \PY{n}{band}\PY{o}{.}\PY{n}{max}\PY{p}{(}\PY{p}{)}
    \PY{k}{if} \PY{n}{b\PYZus{}max} \PY{o}{==} \PY{n}{b\PYZus{}min}\PY{p}{:}
        \PY{k}{return} \PY{n}{np}\PY{o}{.}\PY{n}{zeros\PYZus{}like}\PY{p}{(}\PY{n}{band}\PY{p}{,} \PY{n}{dtype}\PY{o}{=}\PY{n}{np}\PY{o}{.}\PY{n}{float32}\PY{p}{)}
    \PY{k}{return} \PY{p}{(}\PY{n}{band} \PY{o}{\PYZhy{}} \PY{n}{b\PYZus{}min}\PY{p}{)}\PY{o}{.}\PY{n}{astype}\PY{p}{(}\PY{n}{np}\PY{o}{.}\PY{n}{float32}\PY{p}{)} \PY{o}{/} \PY{p}{(}\PY{n}{b\PYZus{}max} \PY{o}{\PYZhy{}} \PY{n}{b\PYZus{}min}\PY{p}{)}


\PY{k}{def}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n+nf}{label\PYZus{}to\PYZus{}rgb}\PY{p}{(}\PY{n}{label\PYZus{}band}\PY{p}{,} \PY{n}{colors}\PY{p}{)}\PY{p}{:}
\PY{+w}{    }\PY{l+s+sd}{\PYZdq{}\PYZdq{}\PYZdq{}}
\PY{l+s+sd}{    Converts an integer label map (H, W) to an RGB image (H, W, 3).}

\PY{l+s+sd}{    Each unique integer gets painted with the corresponding colour.}

\PY{l+s+sd}{    Args:}
\PY{l+s+sd}{        label\PYZus{}band : 2D numpy array of class IDs (integers 0\PYZhy{}5)}
\PY{l+s+sd}{        colors     : list of [R, G, B] colour triplets}
\PY{l+s+sd}{    Returns:}
\PY{l+s+sd}{        rgb : uint8 array of shape (H, W, 3)}
\PY{l+s+sd}{    \PYZdq{}\PYZdq{}\PYZdq{}}
    \PY{n}{h}\PY{p}{,} \PY{n}{w} \PY{o}{=} \PY{n}{label\PYZus{}band}\PY{o}{.}\PY{n}{shape}
    \PY{n}{rgb}  \PY{o}{=} \PY{n}{np}\PY{o}{.}\PY{n}{zeros}\PY{p}{(}\PY{p}{(}\PY{n}{h}\PY{p}{,} \PY{n}{w}\PY{p}{,} \PY{l+m+mi}{3}\PY{p}{)}\PY{p}{,} \PY{n}{dtype}\PY{o}{=}\PY{n}{np}\PY{o}{.}\PY{n}{uint8}\PY{p}{)}  \PY{c+c1}{\PYZsh{} start: black image}

    \PY{k}{for} \PY{n}{class\PYZus{}idx}\PY{p}{,} \PY{n}{color} \PY{o+ow}{in} \PY{n+nb}{enumerate}\PY{p}{(}\PY{n}{colors}\PY{p}{)}\PY{p}{:}
        \PY{n}{mask}       \PY{o}{=} \PY{p}{(}\PY{n}{label\PYZus{}band} \PY{o}{==} \PY{n}{class\PYZus{}idx}\PY{p}{)}  \PY{c+c1}{\PYZsh{} boolean mask for this class}
        \PY{n}{rgb}\PY{p}{[}\PY{n}{mask}\PY{p}{]}  \PY{o}{=} \PY{n}{color}                       \PY{c+c1}{\PYZsh{} paint all those pixels}

    \PY{k}{return} \PY{n}{rgb}


\PY{c+c1}{\PYZsh{} ── Select a sample tile ─────────────────────────────────────────────}
\PY{n}{EXCLUDED}   \PY{o}{=} \PY{l+s+s1}{\PYZsq{}}\PY{l+s+s1}{0000000224\PYZhy{}0000042784.tif}\PY{l+s+s1}{\PYZsq{}}   \PY{c+c1}{\PYZsh{} example tile from the assignment}
\PY{n}{candidates} \PY{o}{=} \PY{p}{[}\PY{n}{f} \PY{k}{for} \PY{n}{f} \PY{o+ow}{in} \PY{n}{all\PYZus{}files} \PY{k}{if} \PY{n}{EXCLUDED} \PY{o+ow}{not} \PY{o+ow}{in} \PY{n}{f}\PY{p}{]}
\PY{n}{sample\PYZus{}file} \PY{o}{=} \PY{n}{random}\PY{o}{.}\PY{n}{choice}\PY{p}{(}\PY{n}{candidates}\PY{p}{)} \PY{k}{if} \PY{n}{candidates} \PY{k}{else} \PY{n}{all\PYZus{}files}\PY{p}{[}\PY{l+m+mi}{0}\PY{p}{]}
\PY{n+nb}{print}\PY{p}{(}\PY{l+s+s1}{\PYZsq{}}\PY{l+s+s1}{Selected tile:}\PY{l+s+s1}{\PYZsq{}}\PY{p}{,} \PY{n}{os}\PY{o}{.}\PY{n}{path}\PY{o}{.}\PY{n}{basename}\PY{p}{(}\PY{n}{sample\PYZus{}file}\PY{p}{)}\PY{p}{)}

\PY{c+c1}{\PYZsh{} ── Read the tile ────────────────────────────────────────────────────}
\PY{k}{with} \PY{n}{rasterio}\PY{o}{.}\PY{n}{open}\PY{p}{(}\PY{n}{sample\PYZus{}file}\PY{p}{)} \PY{k}{as} \PY{n}{src}\PY{p}{:}
    \PY{n}{data}      \PY{o}{=} \PY{n}{src}\PY{o}{.}\PY{n}{read}\PY{p}{(}\PY{p}{)}       \PY{c+c1}{\PYZsh{} shape: (6, H, W)}
    \PY{n}{crs}       \PY{o}{=} \PY{n}{src}\PY{o}{.}\PY{n}{crs}
    \PY{n}{transform} \PY{o}{=} \PY{n}{src}\PY{o}{.}\PY{n}{transform}

\PY{n+nb}{print}\PY{p}{(}\PY{l+s+sa}{f}\PY{l+s+s1}{\PYZsq{}}\PY{l+s+se}{\PYZbs{}n}\PY{l+s+s1}{Data shape : }\PY{l+s+si}{\PYZob{}}\PY{n}{data}\PY{o}{.}\PY{n}{shape}\PY{l+s+si}{\PYZcb{}}\PY{l+s+s1}{  →  }\PY{l+s+si}{\PYZob{}}\PY{n}{data}\PY{o}{.}\PY{n}{shape}\PY{p}{[}\PY{l+m+mi}{0}\PY{p}{]}\PY{l+s+si}{\PYZcb{}}\PY{l+s+s1}{ bands | }\PY{l+s+si}{\PYZob{}}\PY{n}{data}\PY{o}{.}\PY{n}{shape}\PY{p}{[}\PY{l+m+mi}{1}\PY{p}{]}\PY{l+s+si}{\PYZcb{}}\PY{l+s+s1}{×}\PY{l+s+si}{\PYZob{}}\PY{n}{data}\PY{o}{.}\PY{n}{shape}\PY{p}{[}\PY{l+m+mi}{2}\PY{p}{]}\PY{l+s+si}{\PYZcb{}}\PY{l+s+s1}{ px}\PY{l+s+s1}{\PYZsq{}}\PY{p}{)}
\PY{n+nb}{print}\PY{p}{(}\PY{l+s+sa}{f}\PY{l+s+s1}{\PYZsq{}}\PY{l+s+s1}{CRS        : }\PY{l+s+si}{\PYZob{}}\PY{n}{crs}\PY{l+s+si}{\PYZcb{}}\PY{l+s+s1}{\PYZsq{}}\PY{p}{)}
\PY{n+nb}{print}\PY{p}{(}\PY{l+s+sa}{f}\PY{l+s+s1}{\PYZsq{}}\PY{l+s+se}{\PYZbs{}n}\PY{l+s+s1}{Band value ranges:}\PY{l+s+s1}{\PYZsq{}}\PY{p}{)}
\PY{k}{for} \PY{n}{i}\PY{p}{,} \PY{n}{name} \PY{o+ow}{in} \PY{n+nb}{enumerate}\PY{p}{(}\PY{p}{[}\PY{l+s+s1}{\PYZsq{}}\PY{l+s+s1}{Red}\PY{l+s+s1}{\PYZsq{}}\PY{p}{,}\PY{l+s+s1}{\PYZsq{}}\PY{l+s+s1}{Green}\PY{l+s+s1}{\PYZsq{}}\PY{p}{,}\PY{l+s+s1}{\PYZsq{}}\PY{l+s+s1}{Blue}\PY{l+s+s1}{\PYZsq{}}\PY{p}{,}\PY{l+s+s1}{\PYZsq{}}\PY{l+s+s1}{Infrared}\PY{l+s+s1}{\PYZsq{}}\PY{p}{,}\PY{l+s+s1}{\PYZsq{}}\PY{l+s+s1}{Elevation}\PY{l+s+s1}{\PYZsq{}}\PY{p}{,}\PY{l+s+s1}{\PYZsq{}}\PY{l+s+s1}{Labels}\PY{l+s+s1}{\PYZsq{}}\PY{p}{]}\PY{p}{)}\PY{p}{:}
    \PY{n+nb}{print}\PY{p}{(}\PY{l+s+sa}{f}\PY{l+s+s1}{\PYZsq{}}\PY{l+s+s1}{  Band }\PY{l+s+si}{\PYZob{}}\PY{n}{i}\PY{l+s+si}{\PYZcb{}}\PY{l+s+s1}{ (}\PY{l+s+si}{\PYZob{}}\PY{n}{name}\PY{l+s+si}{:}\PY{l+s+s1}{\PYZlt{}12}\PY{l+s+si}{\PYZcb{}}\PY{l+s+s1}{): }\PY{l+s+si}{\PYZob{}}\PY{n}{data}\PY{p}{[}\PY{n}{i}\PY{p}{]}\PY{o}{.}\PY{n}{min}\PY{p}{(}\PY{p}{)}\PY{l+s+si}{:}\PY{l+s+s1}{6}\PY{l+s+si}{\PYZcb{}}\PY{l+s+s1}{ → }\PY{l+s+si}{\PYZob{}}\PY{n}{data}\PY{p}{[}\PY{n}{i}\PY{p}{]}\PY{o}{.}\PY{n}{max}\PY{p}{(}\PY{p}{)}\PY{l+s+si}{:}\PY{l+s+s1}{6}\PY{l+s+si}{\PYZcb{}}\PY{l+s+s1}{\PYZsq{}}\PY{p}{)}
\end{Verbatim}
\end{tcolorbox}

    \begin{Verbatim}[commandchars=\\\{\}]

    \subsubsection{1.3 --- Visualising Three
Views}\label{visualising-three-views}

\paragraph{Why three views?}\label{why-three-views}

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  \textbf{RGB image}: what the human eye sees
\item
  \textbf{Elevation band}: buildings are taller than the ground ---
  clearly visible
\item
  \textbf{Label map}: what our model must learn to predict
\end{itemize}

\paragraph{\texorpdfstring{\texttt{np.stack}}{np.stack}}\label{np.stack}

Stacks three 2D arrays into one 3D array:

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\NormalTok{stack([R(}\DecValTok{224}\NormalTok{,}\DecValTok{224}\NormalTok{), G(}\DecValTok{224}\NormalTok{,}\DecValTok{224}\NormalTok{), B(}\DecValTok{224}\NormalTok{,}\DecValTok{224}\NormalTok{)], axis}\OperatorTok{={-}}\DecValTok{1}\NormalTok{) → (}\DecValTok{224}\NormalTok{, }\DecValTok{224}\NormalTok{, }\DecValTok{3}\NormalTok{)}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

\paragraph{\texorpdfstring{\texttt{plt.subplots(1,\ 3)}}{plt.subplots(1, 3)}}\label{plt.subplots1-3}

Creates a figure with \textbf{1 row} and \textbf{3 columns} --- three
plots side by side.

\paragraph{\texorpdfstring{\texttt{plt.colorbar}}{plt.colorbar}}\label{plt.colorbar}

Colour bar beside the elevation plot: brighter colour = higher
elevation.

\paragraph{\texorpdfstring{\texttt{mpatches.Patch}}{mpatches.Patch}}\label{mpatches.patch}

Creates coloured legend keys (one per class) so we know what each colour
means.

    \subsubsection{1.4 --- Class Distribution
Statistics}\label{class-distribution-statistics}

\paragraph{Why check class
distribution?}\label{why-check-class-distribution}

Suppose 90\% of pixels are `Impervious surface' and 1\% are `Car'. A
model that always predicts `Impervious surface' has 90\% accuracy ---
but is \textbf{useless}!

Checking distribution tells us: - Whether the dataset is
\textbf{class-balanced} - Which classes will be easier/harder to learn -
Whether we need class-weighted loss later

\paragraph{How it works:}\label{how-it-works}

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\NormalTok{(label }\OperatorTok{==}\NormalTok{ i).}\BuiltInTok{sum}\NormalTok{()   →  boolean mask → count of }\VariableTok{True}\NormalTok{ values }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ pixel count }\ControlFlowTok{for} \KeywordTok{class}\NormalTok{ i}
\NormalTok{count }\OperatorTok{/}\NormalTok{ total }\OperatorTok{*} \DecValTok{100}\NormalTok{  →  percentage}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

    \begin{tcolorbox}[breakable, size=fbox, boxrule=1pt, pad at break*=1mm,colback=cellbackground, colframe=cellborder]
\prompt{In}{incolor}{11}{\boxspacing}
\begin{Verbatim}[commandchars=\\\{\}]

\PY{n+nb}{print}\PY{p}{(}\PY{l+s+s1}{\PYZsq{}}\PY{l+s+s1}{Class distribution in sample tile:}\PY{l+s+s1}{\PYZsq{}}\PY{p}{)}
\PY{n+nb}{print}\PY{p}{(}\PY{l+s+sa}{f}\PY{l+s+s1}{\PYZsq{}}\PY{l+s+si}{\PYZob{}}\PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{l+s+s2}{Class}\PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{l+s+si}{:}\PY{l+s+s1}{\PYZlt{}30}\PY{l+s+si}{\PYZcb{}}\PY{l+s+s1}{ }\PY{l+s+si}{\PYZob{}}\PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{l+s+s2}{Pixels}\PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{l+s+si}{:}\PY{l+s+s1}{\PYZgt{}12}\PY{l+s+si}{\PYZcb{}}\PY{l+s+s1}{ }\PY{l+s+si}{\PYZob{}}\PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{l+s+s2}{Percent}\PY{l+s+s2}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{l+s+si}{:}\PY{l+s+s1}{\PYZgt{}9}\PY{l+s+si}{\PYZcb{}}\PY{l+s+s1}{  Bar}\PY{l+s+s1}{\PYZsq{}}\PY{p}{)}
\PY{n+nb}{print}\PY{p}{(}\PY{l+s+s1}{\PYZsq{}}\PY{l+s+s1}{=}\PY{l+s+s1}{\PYZsq{}} \PY{o}{*} \PY{l+m+mi}{68}\PY{p}{)}

\PY{k}{for} \PY{n}{i}\PY{p}{,} \PY{n}{name} \PY{o+ow}{in} \PY{n+nb}{enumerate}\PY{p}{(}\PY{n}{CLASS\PYZus{}NAMES}\PY{p}{)}\PY{p}{:}
    \PY{n}{count}   \PY{o}{=} \PY{p}{(}\PY{n}{label} \PY{o}{==} \PY{n}{i}\PY{p}{)}\PY{o}{.}\PY{n}{sum}\PY{p}{(}\PY{p}{)}

    \PY{n}{bar}     \PY{o}{=} \PY{l+s+s1}{\PYZsq{}}\PY{l+s+s1}{|}\PY{l+s+s1}{\PYZsq{}} \PY{o}{+} \PY{l+s+s1}{\PYZsq{}}\PY{l+s+s1}{█}\PY{l+s+s1}{\PYZsq{}} \PY{o}{*} \PY{n+nb}{int}\PY{p}{(}\PY{n}{pct} \PY{o}{/} \PY{l+m+mi}{2}\PY{p}{)}
    \PY{n+nb}{print}\PY{p}{(}\PY{l+s+sa}{f}\PY{l+s+s1}{\PYZsq{}}\PY{l+s+si}{\PYZob{}}\PY{n}{name}\PY{l+s+si}{:}\PY{l+s+s1}{\PYZlt{}30}\PY{l+s+si}{\PYZcb{}}\PY{l+s+s1}{ }\PY{l+s+si}{\PYZob{}}\PY{n}{count}\PY{l+s+si}{:}\PY{l+s+s1}{\PYZgt{}12,}\PY{l+s+si}{\PYZcb{}}\PY{l+s+s1}{ }\PY{l+s+si}{\PYZob{}}\PY{n}{pct}\PY{l+s+si}{:}\PY{l+s+s1}{\PYZgt{}8.2f}\PY{l+s+si}{\PYZcb{}}\PY{l+s+s1}{\PYZpc{}  }\PY{l+s+si}{\PYZob{}}\PY{n}{bar}\PY{l+s+si}{\PYZcb{}}\PY{l+s+s1}{\PYZsq{}}\PY{p}{)}

\PY{n+nb}{print}\PY{p}{(}\PY{l+s+s1}{\PYZsq{}}\PY{l+s+s1}{=}\PY{l+s+s1}{\PYZsq{}} \PY{o}{*} \PY{l+m+mi}{68}\PY{p}{)}
\PY{n+nb}{print}\PY{p}{(}\PY{l+s+sa}{f}\PY{l+s+s1}{\PYZsq{}}\PY{l+s+s1}{Total: }\PY{l+s+si}{\PYZob{}}\PY{n}{total\PYZus{}pixels}\PY{l+s+si}{:}\PY{l+s+s1}{,}\PY{l+s+si}{\PYZcb{}}\PY{l+s+s1}{ pixels  (}\PY{l+s+si}{\PYZob{}}\PY{n}{data}\PY{o}{.}\PY{n}{shape}\PY{p}{[}\PY{l+m+mi}{1}\PY{p}{]}\PY{l+s+si}{\PYZcb{}}\PY{l+s+s1}{ × }\PY{l+s+si}{\PYZob{}}\PY{n}{data}\PY{o}{.}\PY{n}{shape}\PY{p}{[}\PY{l+m+mi}{2}\PY{p}{]}\PY{l+s+si}{\PYZcb{}}\PY{l+s+s1}{)}\PY{l+s+s1}{\PYZsq{}}\PY{p}{)}

\PY{n}{dominant} \PY{o}{=} \PY{n+nb}{max}\PY{p}{(}\PY{n+nb}{range}\PY{p}{(}\PY{n}{NUM\PYZus{}CLASSES}\PY{p}{)}\PY{p}{,} \PY{n}{key}\PY{o}{=}\PY{k}{lambda} \PY{n}{i}\PY{p}{:} \PY{p}{(}\PY{n}{label}\PY{o}{==}\PY{n}{i}\PY{p}{)}\PY{o}{.}\PY{n}{sum}\PY{p}{(}\PY{p}{)}\PY{p}{)}
\PY{n+nb}{print}\PY{p}{(}\PY{l+s+sa}{f}\PY{l+s+s1}{\PYZsq{}}\PY{l+s+se}{\PYZbs{}n}\PY{l+s+s1}{Dominant class: }\PY{l+s+s1}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{l+s+si}{\PYZob{}}\PY{n}{CLASS\PYZus{}NAMES}\PY{p}{[}\PY{n}{dominant}\PY{p}{]}\PY{l+s+si}{\PYZcb{}}\PY{l+s+s1}{\PYZdq{}}\PY{l+s+s1}{ at }\PY{l+s+si}{\PYZob{}}\PY{l+m+mi}{100}\PY{o}{*}\PY{p}{(}\PY{n}{label}\PY{o}{==}\PY{n}{dominant}\PY{p}{)}\PY{o}{.}\PY{n}{sum}\PY{p}{(}\PY{p}{)}\PY{o}{/}\PY{n}{total\PYZus{}pixels}\PY{l+s+si}{:}\PY{l+s+s1}{.1f}\PY{l+s+si}{\PYZcb{}}\PY{l+s+s1}{\PYZpc{}}\PY{l+s+s1}{\PYZsq{}}\PY{p}{)}
\end{Verbatim}
\end{tcolorbox}

    \subsubsection{1.5 --- K-Fold Cross-Validation
Split}\label{k-fold-cross-validation-split}

\paragraph{The evaluation problem}\label{the-evaluation-problem}

If we evaluate on the \textbf{same data we trained on}, it's like giving
a student the exam questions the night before. The score looks great but
means nothing.

\paragraph{Solution: K-Fold
Cross-Validation}\label{solution-k-fold-cross-validation}

Split all data into \textbf{K equal groups (folds)}:

\begin{verbatim}
All files: ████████████████████ (100 files)
            ├─ Fold 1 ─┤├─ Fold 2 ─┤├─ Fold 3 ─┤├─ Fold 4 ─┤├─ Fold 5 ─┤
                   Training (60%)          Validation (20%)   Test (20%)
                  Folds 1 + 2 + 3              Fold 4          Fold 5
\end{verbatim}

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  \textbf{Train (Folds 1+2+3)}: model learns from these
\item
  \textbf{Validation (Fold 4)}: monitored during training --- used to
  save the best model
\item
  \textbf{Test (Fold 5)}: never seen during training --- the true final
  evaluation
\end{itemize}

\paragraph{\texorpdfstring{\texttt{KFold(n\_splits=5)}}{KFold(n\_splits=5)}}\label{kfoldn_splits5}

scikit-learn's ready-made splitter: - shuffles indices (with
\texttt{shuffle=True}) for fairness - yields 5 (train\_indices,
test\_indices) pairs

    \begin{tcolorbox}[breakable, size=fbox, boxrule=1pt, pad at break*=1mm,colback=cellbackground, colframe=cellborder]
\prompt{In}{incolor}{12}{\boxspacing}
\begin{Verbatim}[commandchars=\\\{\}]
\PY{c+c1}{\PYZsh{} ── Step 1c: K\PYZhy{}Fold Cross\PYZhy{}Validation Split ──────────────────────────}

\PY{c+c1}{\PYZsh{} With only a few files, repeat them to demonstrate the split mechanism}
\PY{n}{demo\PYZus{}files} \PY{o}{=} \PY{n}{all\PYZus{}files} \PY{o}{*} \PY{n+nb}{max}\PY{p}{(}\PY{l+m+mi}{1}\PY{p}{,} \PY{p}{(}\PY{n}{N\PYZus{}FOLDS} \PY{o}{*} \PY{l+m+mi}{4}\PY{p}{)} \PY{o}{/}\PY{o}{/} \PY{n+nb}{max}\PY{p}{(}\PY{n+nb}{len}\PY{p}{(}\PY{n}{all\PYZus{}files}\PY{p}{)}\PY{p}{,} \PY{l+m+mi}{1}\PY{p}{)} \PY{o}{+} \PY{l+m+mi}{1}\PY{p}{)}
\PY{n}{demo\PYZus{}files} \PY{o}{=} \PY{n}{demo\PYZus{}files}\PY{p}{[}\PY{p}{:}\PY{n+nb}{max}\PY{p}{(}\PY{n+nb}{len}\PY{p}{(}\PY{n}{all\PYZus{}files}\PY{p}{)}\PY{p}{,} \PY{n}{N\PYZus{}FOLDS} \PY{o}{*} \PY{l+m+mi}{4}\PY{p}{)}\PY{p}{]}

\PY{n}{kf}  \PY{o}{=} \PY{n}{KFold}\PY{p}{(}\PY{n}{n\PYZus{}splits}\PY{o}{=}\PY{n}{N\PYZus{}FOLDS}\PY{p}{,} \PY{n}{shuffle}\PY{o}{=}\PY{k+kc}{True}\PY{p}{,} \PY{n}{random\PYZus{}state}\PY{o}{=}\PY{n}{SEED}\PY{p}{)}
\PY{n}{arr} \PY{o}{=} \PY{n}{np}\PY{o}{.}\PY{n}{array}\PY{p}{(}\PY{n}{demo\PYZus{}files}\PY{p}{)}
\PY{n}{folds} \PY{o}{=} \PY{p}{[}\PY{n}{arr}\PY{p}{[}\PY{n}{idx}\PY{p}{]}\PY{o}{.}\PY{n}{tolist}\PY{p}{(}\PY{p}{)} \PY{k}{for} \PY{n}{\PYZus{}}\PY{p}{,} \PY{n}{idx} \PY{o+ow}{in} \PY{n}{kf}\PY{o}{.}\PY{n}{split}\PY{p}{(}\PY{n}{arr}\PY{p}{)}\PY{p}{]}

\PY{n}{train\PYZus{}files} \PY{o}{=} \PY{n}{folds}\PY{p}{[}\PY{l+m+mi}{0}\PY{p}{]} \PY{o}{+} \PY{n}{folds}\PY{p}{[}\PY{l+m+mi}{1}\PY{p}{]} \PY{o}{+} \PY{n}{folds}\PY{p}{[}\PY{l+m+mi}{2}\PY{p}{]}   \PY{c+c1}{\PYZsh{} Folds 1, 2, 3 → Training}
\PY{n}{val\PYZus{}files}   \PY{o}{=} \PY{n}{folds}\PY{p}{[}\PY{l+m+mi}{3}\PY{p}{]}                            \PY{c+c1}{\PYZsh{} Fold 4        → Validation}
\PY{n}{test\PYZus{}files}  \PY{o}{=} \PY{n}{folds}\PY{p}{[}\PY{l+m+mi}{4}\PY{p}{]}                            \PY{c+c1}{\PYZsh{} Fold 5        → Test}

\PY{n+nb}{print}\PY{p}{(}\PY{l+s+s1}{\PYZsq{}}\PY{l+s+s1}{K\PYZhy{}Fold split result:}\PY{l+s+s1}{\PYZsq{}}\PY{p}{)}
\PY{k}{for} \PY{n}{i}\PY{p}{,} \PY{n}{fold} \PY{o+ow}{in} \PY{n+nb}{enumerate}\PY{p}{(}\PY{n}{folds}\PY{p}{,} \PY{l+m+mi}{1}\PY{p}{)}\PY{p}{:}
    \PY{n}{role} \PY{o}{=} \PY{l+s+s1}{\PYZsq{}}\PY{l+s+s1}{Train}\PY{l+s+s1}{\PYZsq{}} \PY{k}{if} \PY{n}{i}\PY{o}{\PYZlt{}}\PY{o}{=}\PY{l+m+mi}{3} \PY{k}{else} \PY{p}{(}\PY{l+s+s1}{\PYZsq{}}\PY{l+s+s1}{Val}\PY{l+s+s1}{\PYZsq{}} \PY{k}{if} \PY{n}{i}\PY{o}{==}\PY{l+m+mi}{4} \PY{k}{else} \PY{l+s+s1}{\PYZsq{}}\PY{l+s+s1}{Test}\PY{l+s+s1}{\PYZsq{}}\PY{p}{)}
    \PY{n+nb}{print}\PY{p}{(}\PY{l+s+sa}{f}\PY{l+s+s1}{\PYZsq{}}\PY{l+s+s1}{  Fold }\PY{l+s+si}{\PYZob{}}\PY{n}{i}\PY{l+s+si}{\PYZcb{}}\PY{l+s+s1}{ (}\PY{l+s+si}{\PYZob{}}\PY{n}{role}\PY{l+s+si}{:}\PY{l+s+s1}{5}\PY{l+s+si}{\PYZcb{}}\PY{l+s+s1}{): }\PY{l+s+si}{\PYZob{}}\PY{n+nb}{len}\PY{p}{(}\PY{n}{fold}\PY{p}{)}\PY{l+s+si}{\PYZcb{}}\PY{l+s+s1}{ files}\PY{l+s+s1}{\PYZsq{}}\PY{p}{)}
\PY{n+nb}{print}\PY{p}{(}\PY{l+s+sa}{f}\PY{l+s+s1}{\PYZsq{}}\PY{l+s+se}{\PYZbs{}n}\PY{l+s+s1}{Total  Train  : }\PY{l+s+si}{\PYZob{}}\PY{n+nb}{len}\PY{p}{(}\PY{n}{train\PYZus{}files}\PY{p}{)}\PY{l+s+si}{\PYZcb{}}\PY{l+s+s1}{ files}\PY{l+s+s1}{\PYZsq{}}\PY{p}{)}
\PY{n+nb}{print}\PY{p}{(}\PY{l+s+sa}{f}\PY{l+s+s1}{\PYZsq{}}\PY{l+s+s1}{Total  Val    : }\PY{l+s+si}{\PYZob{}}\PY{n+nb}{len}\PY{p}{(}\PY{n}{val\PYZus{}files}\PY{p}{)}\PY{l+s+si}{\PYZcb{}}\PY{l+s+s1}{ files}\PY{l+s+s1}{\PYZsq{}}\PY{p}{)}
\PY{n+nb}{print}\PY{p}{(}\PY{l+s+sa}{f}\PY{l+s+s1}{\PYZsq{}}\PY{l+s+s1}{Total  Test   : }\PY{l+s+si}{\PYZob{}}\PY{n+nb}{len}\PY{p}{(}\PY{n}{test\PYZus{}files}\PY{p}{)}\PY{l+s+si}{\PYZcb{}}\PY{l+s+s1}{ files}\PY{l+s+s1}{\PYZsq{}}\PY{p}{)}

\PY{n}{splits\PYZus{}path} \PY{o}{=} \PY{n}{os}\PY{o}{.}\PY{n}{path}\PY{o}{.}\PY{n}{join}\PY{p}{(}\PY{n}{OUTPUT\PYZus{}DIR}\PY{p}{,} \PY{l+s+s1}{\PYZsq{}}\PY{l+s+s1}{fold\PYZus{}splits.json}\PY{l+s+s1}{\PYZsq{}}\PY{p}{)}
\PY{k}{with} \PY{n+nb}{open}\PY{p}{(}\PY{n}{splits\PYZus{}path}\PY{p}{,} \PY{l+s+s1}{\PYZsq{}}\PY{l+s+s1}{w}\PY{l+s+s1}{\PYZsq{}}\PY{p}{)} \PY{k}{as} \PY{n}{fp}\PY{p}{:}
    \PY{n}{json}\PY{o}{.}\PY{n}{dump}\PY{p}{(}\PY{p}{\PYZob{}}\PY{l+s+s1}{\PYZsq{}}\PY{l+s+s1}{train}\PY{l+s+s1}{\PYZsq{}}\PY{p}{:}\PY{n}{train\PYZus{}files}\PY{p}{,}\PY{l+s+s1}{\PYZsq{}}\PY{l+s+s1}{val}\PY{l+s+s1}{\PYZsq{}}\PY{p}{:}\PY{n}{val\PYZus{}files}\PY{p}{,}\PY{l+s+s1}{\PYZsq{}}\PY{l+s+s1}{test}\PY{l+s+s1}{\PYZsq{}}\PY{p}{:}\PY{n}{test\PYZus{}files}\PY{p}{,}\PY{l+s+s1}{\PYZsq{}}\PY{l+s+s1}{all\PYZus{}folds}\PY{l+s+s1}{\PYZsq{}}\PY{p}{:}\PY{n}{folds}\PY{p}{\PYZcb{}}\PY{p}{,} \PY{n}{fp}\PY{p}{,} \PY{n}{indent}\PY{o}{=}\PY{l+m+mi}{2}\PY{p}{)}
\PY{n+nb}{print}\PY{p}{(}\PY{l+s+s1}{\PYZsq{}}\PY{l+s+se}{\PYZbs{}n}\PY{l+s+s1}{Saved fold\PYZus{}splits.json}\PY{l+s+s1}{\PYZsq{}}\PY{p}{)}
\end{Verbatim}
\end{tcolorbox}

    \subsubsection{✅ Step 1 Summary}\label{step-1-summary}

\textbf{What we learned:} - GeoTIFF format: 6-band multi-spectral image
- \texttt{normalize\_band}: maps any range → {[}0, 1{]} for display -
\texttt{label\_to\_rgb}: integer class IDs → coloured image - K-Fold CV:
unbiased evaluation strategy

\textbf{Files produced:} - \texttt{step1\_visualization.png} --- RGB /
Elevation / Label visualisation - \texttt{fold\_splits.json} ---
train/val/test file lists

\textbf{→ Next: Step 2 --- Build and train our first neural network!}

    \begin{center}\rule{0.5\linewidth}{0.5pt}\end{center}

\section{━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━}\label{section}

\section{Step 2: Simple Convolutional Neural Network
(CNN)}\label{step-2-simple-convolutional-neural-network-cnn}

\section{━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━}\label{section-1}

\subsection{Core Concept: What is a Convolutional Neural
Network?}\label{core-concept-what-is-a-convolutional-neural-network}

\subsubsection{The problem with Fully-Connected networks on
images}\label{the-problem-with-fully-connected-networks-on-images}

In a standard Fully-Connected (Dense) network every neuron sees every
pixel: - 224×224 image → 50,176 input values - First layer of 1,000
neurons → \textbf{50 million weights} for \emph{one layer!} - Slow,
memory-hungry, and overfits easily

\subsubsection{Solution: Convolution}\label{solution-convolution}

A \textbf{convolution} slides a small filter (e.g.~3×3) across the
entire image:

\begin{verbatim}
Input image:              3×3 Filter:       Feature Map (output):
┌─────────────────┐       ┌───────┐          ┌─────────────────┐
│ 1  2  3  4  5  │       │ 1 0 -1│          │ value  value …  │
│ 6  7  8  9  10 │   ×   │ 2 0 -2│   →  →   │ …               │
│ 11 12 13 14 15 │       │ 1 0 -1│          │                 │
│ …               │       └───────┘          └─────────────────┘
└─────────────────┘
\end{verbatim}

\textbf{Advantages:} - A 3×3 filter has only \textbf{9 weights} (not
millions) - The \emph{same} filter is reused across the entire image
(weight sharing) - Spatial structure is preserved

\subsubsection{Our model: Fully Convolutional
Network}\label{our-model-fully-convolutional-network}

\begin{verbatim}
Input (H×W×4)
    ↓
[Conv 3×3 × 32] + BN + ReLU   ← Block 1: simple features (edges, colours)
[Conv 3×3 × 32] + BN + ReLU
    ↓
[Conv 3×3 × 64] + BN + ReLU   ← Block 2: complex features (textures)
[Conv 3×3 × 64] + BN + ReLU
    ↓
[Conv 3×3 × 128] + BN + ReLU  ← Block 3: abstract features (shapes)
[Conv 3×3 × 128] + BN + ReLU
    ↓
[Conv 1×1 × 6]                 ← Output: 6 channels (one per class)
    ↓
Softmax                         ← per-pixel class probabilities
Output (H×W×6)
\end{verbatim}

\textbf{Key note:} No MaxPooling → image size stays constant. Every
input pixel maps directly to a class-probability vector.

    \subsubsection{Key Layer Concepts}\label{key-layer-concepts}

\paragraph{BatchNormalization}\label{batchnormalization}

During training, intermediate values can grow very large or small.
BatchNorm \textbf{standardises} them (mean ≈ 0, variance ≈ 1) after each
layer.

→ More stable training, faster convergence, acts as a mild regulariser.

\paragraph{ReLU (Rectified Linear
Unit)}\label{relu-rectified-linear-unit}

\begin{verbatim}
ReLU(x) = max(0, x)
\end{verbatim}

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  Negative values → clamped to 0
\item
  Positive values → unchanged
\end{itemize}

\textbf{Why?} Without a non-linearity, stacking any number of linear
layers is still just one linear function --- useless for complex
patterns.

\paragraph{Softmax}\label{softmax}

\begin{verbatim}
softmax([2.0, 1.0, 0.5]) → [0.66, 0.24, 0.10]   (sums to 1)
\end{verbatim}

Converts raw scores into \textbf{probabilities} that sum to 1. For each
pixel: probability of belonging to each of the 6 classes.

\paragraph{Conv2D(filters=6, kernel\_size=1) --- the 1×1
convolution}\label{conv2dfilters6-kernel_size1-the-11-convolution}

Acts as a \textbf{per-pixel channel reducer}: - Input: (H, W, 128
channels) - Output: (H, W, 6 channels) - No spatial mixing --- just a
learned linear combination of channels

    \subsubsection{2.1 --- Data Loading
Function}\label{data-loading-function}

\paragraph{Why a loading function?}\label{why-a-loading-function}

With thousands of tiles we cannot hold all data in RAM simultaneously.
We load each batch on demand.

\paragraph{\texorpdfstring{What does \texttt{load\_sample}
do?}{What does load\_sample do?}}\label{what-does-load_sample-do}

\begin{enumerate}
\def\labelenumi{\arabic{enumi}.}
\tightlist
\item
  Open the GeoTIFF with \texttt{rasterio}
\item
  Separate input bands (RGB+IR or RGB+IR+Elevation)
\item
  Normalise every band to {[}0, 1{]}
\item
  Convert labels to \textbf{One-Hot encoding}
\end{enumerate}

\paragraph{What is One-Hot encoding?}\label{what-is-one-hot-encoding}

Instead of the class integer (e.g.~4 for Car), we use a 6-element
vector:

\begin{verbatim}
Class 4 (Car)  → [0, 0, 0, 0, 1, 0]
Class 2 (Tree) → [0, 0, 1, 0, 0, 0]
\end{verbatim}

Why? The Categorical Cross-Entropy loss function requires this format.

\paragraph{\texorpdfstring{\texttt{data.transpose(1,\ 2,\ 0)}
explained}{data.transpose(1, 2, 0) explained}}\label{data.transpose1-2-0-explained}

\texttt{rasterio} returns shape \texttt{(bands,\ H,\ W)}.
TensorFlow/Keras expects \texttt{(H,\ W,\ bands)}.
\texttt{transpose(1,\ 2,\ 0)} re-orders the axes accordingly:

\begin{verbatim}
(6, 224, 224)  →  (224, 224, 6)
\end{verbatim}

    \begin{tcolorbox}[breakable, size=fbox, boxrule=1pt, pad at break*=1mm,colback=cellbackground, colframe=cellborder]
\prompt{In}{incolor}{13}{\boxspacing}
\begin{Verbatim}[commandchars=\\\{\}]
\PY{c+c1}{\PYZsh{} ── Step 2 hyper\PYZhy{}parameters ─────────────────────────────────────────}
\PY{n}{BATCH\PYZus{}SIZE} \PY{o}{=} \PY{l+m+mi}{8}       \PY{c+c1}{\PYZsh{} samples per batch (increase with a larger GPU)}
\PY{n}{EPOCHS\PYZus{}CNN} \PY{o}{=} \PY{l+m+mi}{20}      \PY{c+c1}{\PYZsh{} number of full passes over training data}
\PY{n}{LR\PYZus{}CNN}     \PY{o}{=} \PY{l+m+mf}{1e\PYZhy{}3}    \PY{c+c1}{\PYZsh{} learning rate: 0.001}

\PY{k}{def}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n+nf}{load\PYZus{}sample}\PY{p}{(}\PY{n}{file\PYZus{}path}\PY{p}{,} \PY{n}{use\PYZus{}elevation}\PY{o}{=}\PY{k+kc}{False}\PY{p}{)}\PY{p}{:}
\PY{+w}{    }\PY{l+s+sd}{\PYZdq{}\PYZdq{}\PYZdq{}}
\PY{l+s+sd}{    Read one GeoTIFF tile and return a (features, labels) pair.}

\PY{l+s+sd}{    Args:}
\PY{l+s+sd}{        file\PYZus{}path     (str)  : path to a .tif file}
\PY{l+s+sd}{        use\PYZus{}elevation (bool) : True → 5 bands (RGB+IR+Elev)}
\PY{l+s+sd}{                               False → 4 bands (RGB+IR)}
\PY{l+s+sd}{    Returns:}
\PY{l+s+sd}{        X : float32 array of shape (H, W, n\PYZus{}bands), range [0, 1]}
\PY{l+s+sd}{        y : float32 one\PYZhy{}hot array of shape (H, W, NUM\PYZus{}CLASSES)}
\PY{l+s+sd}{    \PYZdq{}\PYZdq{}\PYZdq{}}
    \PY{k}{with} \PY{n}{rasterio}\PY{o}{.}\PY{n}{open}\PY{p}{(}\PY{n}{file\PYZus{}path}\PY{p}{)} \PY{k}{as} \PY{n}{src}\PY{p}{:}
        \PY{n}{d} \PY{o}{=} \PY{n}{src}\PY{o}{.}\PY{n}{read}\PY{p}{(}\PY{p}{)}   \PY{c+c1}{\PYZsh{} (6, H, W)}

    \PY{n}{n\PYZus{}bands} \PY{o}{=} \PY{l+m+mi}{5} \PY{k}{if} \PY{n}{use\PYZus{}elevation} \PY{k}{else} \PY{l+m+mi}{4}

    \PY{c+c1}{\PYZsh{} Reorder axes: (6, H, W) → (H, W, n\PYZus{}bands)}
    \PY{n}{X} \PY{o}{=} \PY{n}{d}\PY{p}{[}\PY{p}{:}\PY{n}{n\PYZus{}bands}\PY{p}{]}\PY{o}{.}\PY{n}{transpose}\PY{p}{(}\PY{l+m+mi}{1}\PY{p}{,} \PY{l+m+mi}{2}\PY{p}{,} \PY{l+m+mi}{0}\PY{p}{)}\PY{o}{.}\PY{n}{astype}\PY{p}{(}\PY{n}{np}\PY{o}{.}\PY{n}{float32}\PY{p}{)}

    \PY{c+c1}{\PYZsh{} Normalise each band independently to [0, 1]}
    \PY{k}{for} \PY{n}{c} \PY{o+ow}{in} \PY{n+nb}{range}\PY{p}{(}\PY{n}{X}\PY{o}{.}\PY{n}{shape}\PY{p}{[}\PY{o}{\PYZhy{}}\PY{l+m+mi}{1}\PY{p}{]}\PY{p}{)}\PY{p}{:}
        \PY{n}{X}\PY{p}{[}\PY{o}{.}\PY{o}{.}\PY{o}{.}\PY{p}{,} \PY{n}{c}\PY{p}{]} \PY{o}{=} \PY{n}{normalize\PYZus{}band}\PY{p}{(}\PY{n}{X}\PY{p}{[}\PY{o}{.}\PY{o}{.}\PY{o}{.}\PY{p}{,} \PY{n}{c}\PY{p}{]}\PY{p}{)}

    \PY{c+c1}{\PYZsh{} Labels: integer map → one\PYZhy{}hot (H, W, 6)}
    \PY{n}{label\PYZus{}band} \PY{o}{=} \PY{n}{d}\PY{p}{[}\PY{l+m+mi}{5}\PY{p}{]}\PY{o}{.}\PY{n}{astype}\PY{p}{(}\PY{n}{np}\PY{o}{.}\PY{n}{int32}\PY{p}{)}
    \PY{n}{y} \PY{o}{=} \PY{n}{tf}\PY{o}{.}\PY{n}{keras}\PY{o}{.}\PY{n}{utils}\PY{o}{.}\PY{n}{to\PYZus{}categorical}\PY{p}{(}\PY{n}{label\PYZus{}band}\PY{p}{,} \PY{n}{num\PYZus{}classes}\PY{o}{=}\PY{n}{NUM\PYZus{}CLASSES}\PY{p}{)}

    \PY{k}{return} \PY{n}{X}\PY{p}{,} \PY{n}{y}

\PY{c+c1}{\PYZsh{} Quick sanity test}
\PY{n}{X4}\PY{p}{,} \PY{n}{y} \PY{o}{=} \PY{n}{load\PYZus{}sample}\PY{p}{(}\PY{n}{sample\PYZus{}file}\PY{p}{,} \PY{n}{use\PYZus{}elevation}\PY{o}{=}\PY{k+kc}{False}\PY{p}{)}
\PY{n}{X5}\PY{p}{,} \PY{n}{\PYZus{}} \PY{o}{=} \PY{n}{load\PYZus{}sample}\PY{p}{(}\PY{n}{sample\PYZus{}file}\PY{p}{,} \PY{n}{use\PYZus{}elevation}\PY{o}{=}\PY{k+kc}{True}\PY{p}{)}
\PY{n+nb}{print}\PY{p}{(}\PY{l+s+s1}{\PYZsq{}}\PY{l+s+s1}{4\PYZhy{}band input (RGB+IR)             :}\PY{l+s+s1}{\PYZsq{}}\PY{p}{,} \PY{n}{X4}\PY{o}{.}\PY{n}{shape}\PY{p}{)}
\PY{n+nb}{print}\PY{p}{(}\PY{l+s+s1}{\PYZsq{}}\PY{l+s+s1}{5\PYZhy{}band input (RGB+IR+Elevation)   :}\PY{l+s+s1}{\PYZsq{}}\PY{p}{,} \PY{n}{X5}\PY{o}{.}\PY{n}{shape}\PY{p}{)}
\PY{n+nb}{print}\PY{p}{(}\PY{l+s+s1}{\PYZsq{}}\PY{l+s+s1}{One\PYZhy{}hot label map                 :}\PY{l+s+s1}{\PYZsq{}}\PY{p}{,} \PY{n}{y}\PY{o}{.}\PY{n}{shape}\PY{p}{)}
\PY{n+nb}{print}\PY{p}{(}\PY{l+s+s1}{\PYZsq{}}\PY{l+s+se}{\PYZbs{}n}\PY{l+s+s1}{One\PYZhy{}hot vector for pixel (0,0)    :}\PY{l+s+s1}{\PYZsq{}}\PY{p}{,} \PY{n}{y}\PY{p}{[}\PY{l+m+mi}{0}\PY{p}{,}\PY{l+m+mi}{0}\PY{p}{,}\PY{p}{:}\PY{p}{]}\PY{p}{)}
\PY{n+nb}{print}\PY{p}{(}\PY{l+s+s1}{\PYZsq{}}\PY{l+s+s1}{→ Predicted class                 :}\PY{l+s+s1}{\PYZsq{}}\PY{p}{,} \PY{n}{CLASS\PYZus{}NAMES}\PY{p}{[}\PY{n}{y}\PY{p}{[}\PY{l+m+mi}{0}\PY{p}{,}\PY{l+m+mi}{0}\PY{p}{,}\PY{p}{:}\PY{p}{]}\PY{o}{.}\PY{n}{argmax}\PY{p}{(}\PY{p}{)}\PY{p}{]}\PY{p}{)}
\end{Verbatim}
\end{tcolorbox}

    \begin{Verbatim}[commandchars=\\\{\}]
4-band input (RGB+IR)             : (224, 224, 4)
5-band input (RGB+IR+Elevation)   : (224, 224, 5)
One-hot label map                 : (224, 224, 6)

One-hot vector for pixel (0,0)    : [0. 0. 0. 1. 0. 0.]
→ Predicted class                 : Low vegetation
    \end{Verbatim}

    \subsubsection{2.2 --- Data Augmentation}\label{data-augmentation}

\paragraph{What is Overfitting?}\label{what-is-overfitting}

\textbf{Overfitting} = the model \textbf{memorises} training examples
instead of \textbf{generalising}.

Example: a student who memorises textbook questions but cannot think
independently.

\textbf{Signs of overfitting in training curves:} - Training Accuracy →
very high (e.g.~99\%) - Validation Accuracy → much lower (e.g.~55\%) -
The \emph{gap} between them keeps growing

\paragraph{Data Augmentation --- the
solution}\label{data-augmentation-the-solution}

Create \textbf{diverse variants} of each image through random geometric
transformations. Like photographing the same scene from different
angles.

\begin{verbatim}
Original:           Horizontal flip:    Vertical flip:     90° rotation:
┌─────────┐         ┌─────────┐         ┌─────────┐        ┌─────────┐
│ 🌳 🏠 🚗│         │🚗 🏠 🌳│         │ 🚗 🏠 🌳│        │   …    │
│ 🛣️  🛣️ │         │ 🛣️  🛣️│         │ 🛣️  🛣️│        │        │
└─────────┘         └─────────┘         └─────────┘        └─────────┘
\end{verbatim}

\textbf{Critical rule:} labels must receive the \emph{exact same}
transformation as the input image, maintaining the pixel-to-class
correspondence.

\paragraph{\texorpdfstring{\texttt{np.rot90(array,\ k)}
explained}{np.rot90(array, k) explained}}\label{np.rot90array-k-explained}

Rotates a 2D array by k × 90°. - k=0: no rotation - k=1: 90°
counter-clockwise - k=2: 180° - k=3: 270°

    \begin{tcolorbox}[breakable, size=fbox, boxrule=1pt, pad at break*=1mm,colback=cellbackground, colframe=cellborder]
\prompt{In}{incolor}{14}{\boxspacing}
\begin{Verbatim}[commandchars=\\\{\}]
\PY{k}{def}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n+nf}{augment}\PY{p}{(}\PY{n}{X}\PY{p}{,} \PY{n}{y}\PY{p}{)}\PY{p}{:}
\PY{+w}{    }\PY{l+s+sd}{\PYZdq{}\PYZdq{}\PYZdq{}}
\PY{l+s+sd}{    Apply random geometric transforms to (input, label) pair.}

\PY{l+s+sd}{    All transforms are applied identically to X and y to preserve}
\PY{l+s+sd}{    the pixel ↔ class correspondence.}

\PY{l+s+sd}{    Transforms applied:}
\PY{l+s+sd}{      • Random horizontal flip   (50\PYZpc{} chance)}
\PY{l+s+sd}{      • Random vertical flip     (50\PYZpc{} chance)}
\PY{l+s+sd}{      • Random 90° rotation      (one of 0°/90°/180°/270°)}

\PY{l+s+sd}{    Args:}
\PY{l+s+sd}{        X : numpy array (H, W, C)   — input features}
\PY{l+s+sd}{        y : numpy array (H, W, 6)  — one\PYZhy{}hot labels}
\PY{l+s+sd}{    Returns:}
\PY{l+s+sd}{        Augmented X and y with the same shape}
\PY{l+s+sd}{    \PYZdq{}\PYZdq{}\PYZdq{}}
    \PY{c+c1}{\PYZsh{} Horizontal flip: reverse columns  [:, ::\PYZhy{}1, :]}
    \PY{k}{if} \PY{n}{np}\PY{o}{.}\PY{n}{random}\PY{o}{.}\PY{n}{rand}\PY{p}{(}\PY{p}{)} \PY{o}{\PYZgt{}} \PY{l+m+mf}{0.5}\PY{p}{:}
        \PY{n}{X} \PY{o}{=} \PY{n}{X}\PY{p}{[}\PY{p}{:}\PY{p}{,} \PY{p}{:}\PY{p}{:}\PY{o}{\PYZhy{}}\PY{l+m+mi}{1}\PY{p}{,} \PY{p}{:}\PY{p}{]}
        \PY{n}{y} \PY{o}{=} \PY{n}{y}\PY{p}{[}\PY{p}{:}\PY{p}{,} \PY{p}{:}\PY{p}{:}\PY{o}{\PYZhy{}}\PY{l+m+mi}{1}\PY{p}{,} \PY{p}{:}\PY{p}{]}

    \PY{c+c1}{\PYZsh{} Vertical flip: reverse rows  [::\PYZhy{}1, :, :]}
    \PY{k}{if} \PY{n}{np}\PY{o}{.}\PY{n}{random}\PY{o}{.}\PY{n}{rand}\PY{p}{(}\PY{p}{)} \PY{o}{\PYZgt{}} \PY{l+m+mf}{0.5}\PY{p}{:}
        \PY{n}{X} \PY{o}{=} \PY{n}{X}\PY{p}{[}\PY{p}{:}\PY{p}{:}\PY{o}{\PYZhy{}}\PY{l+m+mi}{1}\PY{p}{,} \PY{p}{:}\PY{p}{,} \PY{p}{:}\PY{p}{]}
        \PY{n}{y} \PY{o}{=} \PY{n}{y}\PY{p}{[}\PY{p}{:}\PY{p}{:}\PY{o}{\PYZhy{}}\PY{l+m+mi}{1}\PY{p}{,} \PY{p}{:}\PY{p}{,} \PY{p}{:}\PY{p}{]}

    \PY{c+c1}{\PYZsh{} 90° rotation: k ∈ \PYZob{}0, 1, 2, 3\PYZcb{}}
    \PY{n}{k} \PY{o}{=} \PY{n}{np}\PY{o}{.}\PY{n}{random}\PY{o}{.}\PY{n}{randint}\PY{p}{(}\PY{l+m+mi}{0}\PY{p}{,} \PY{l+m+mi}{4}\PY{p}{)}
    \PY{n}{X} \PY{o}{=} \PY{n}{np}\PY{o}{.}\PY{n}{rot90}\PY{p}{(}\PY{n}{X}\PY{p}{,} \PY{n}{k}\PY{p}{)}\PY{o}{.}\PY{n}{copy}\PY{p}{(}\PY{p}{)}   \PY{c+c1}{\PYZsh{} .copy() ensures contiguous memory}
    \PY{n}{y} \PY{o}{=} \PY{n}{np}\PY{o}{.}\PY{n}{rot90}\PY{p}{(}\PY{n}{y}\PY{p}{,} \PY{n}{k}\PY{p}{)}\PY{o}{.}\PY{n}{copy}\PY{p}{(}\PY{p}{)}

    \PY{k}{return} \PY{n}{X}\PY{p}{,} \PY{n}{y}


\PY{k}{def}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n+nf}{make\PYZus{}dataset}\PY{p}{(}\PY{n}{file\PYZus{}list}\PY{p}{,} \PY{n}{augment\PYZus{}data}\PY{o}{=}\PY{k+kc}{False}\PY{p}{,} \PY{n}{batch\PYZus{}size}\PY{o}{=}\PY{l+m+mi}{2}\PY{p}{,} \PY{n}{use\PYZus{}elevation}\PY{o}{=}\PY{k+kc}{False}\PY{p}{)}\PY{p}{:}
\PY{+w}{    }\PY{l+s+sd}{\PYZdq{}\PYZdq{}\PYZdq{}}
\PY{l+s+sd}{    Build a tf.data.Dataset with parallel I/O mapping.}
\PY{l+s+sd}{    This version uses multiple threads to open and process files,}
\PY{l+s+sd}{    eliminating the bottleneck of sequential disk access.}
\PY{l+s+sd}{    \PYZdq{}\PYZdq{}\PYZdq{}}
    \PY{k}{def}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n+nf}{mapper}\PY{p}{(}\PY{n}{fp}\PY{p}{)}\PY{p}{:}
        \PY{c+c1}{\PYZsh{} Convert tensor string to python string}
        \PY{n}{fp\PYZus{}str} \PY{o}{=} \PY{n}{fp}\PY{o}{.}\PY{n}{numpy}\PY{p}{(}\PY{p}{)}\PY{o}{.}\PY{n}{decode}\PY{p}{(}\PY{l+s+s1}{\PYZsq{}}\PY{l+s+s1}{utf\PYZhy{}8}\PY{l+s+s1}{\PYZsq{}}\PY{p}{)}
        \PY{n}{X}\PY{p}{,} \PY{n}{y} \PY{o}{=} \PY{n}{load\PYZus{}sample}\PY{p}{(}\PY{n}{fp\PYZus{}str}\PY{p}{,} \PY{n}{use\PYZus{}elevation}\PY{o}{=}\PY{n}{use\PYZus{}elevation}\PY{p}{)}
        \PY{k}{if} \PY{n}{augment\PYZus{}data}\PY{p}{:}
            \PY{n}{X}\PY{p}{,} \PY{n}{y} \PY{o}{=} \PY{n}{augment}\PY{p}{(}\PY{n}{X}\PY{p}{,} \PY{n}{y}\PY{p}{)}
        \PY{k}{return} \PY{n}{X}\PY{p}{,} \PY{n}{y}

    \PY{c+c1}{\PYZsh{} Helper to wrap the python function for TensorFlow}
    \PY{k}{def}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n+nf}{tf\PYZus{}mapper}\PY{p}{(}\PY{n}{fp}\PY{p}{)}\PY{p}{:}
        \PY{n}{n\PYZus{}bands} \PY{o}{=} \PY{l+m+mi}{5} \PY{k}{if} \PY{n}{use\PYZus{}elevation} \PY{k}{else} \PY{l+m+mi}{4}
        \PY{n}{X}\PY{p}{,} \PY{n}{y} \PY{o}{=} \PY{n}{tf}\PY{o}{.}\PY{n}{py\PYZus{}function}\PY{p}{(}
            \PY{n}{mapper}\PY{p}{,} 
            \PY{p}{[}\PY{n}{fp}\PY{p}{]}\PY{p}{,} 
            \PY{p}{[}\PY{n}{tf}\PY{o}{.}\PY{n}{float32}\PY{p}{,} \PY{n}{tf}\PY{o}{.}\PY{n}{float32}\PY{p}{]}
        \PY{p}{)}
        \PY{c+c1}{\PYZsh{} We must explicitly set shapes after py\PYZus{}function}
        \PY{n}{X}\PY{o}{.}\PY{n}{set\PYZus{}shape}\PY{p}{(}\PY{p}{[}\PY{k+kc}{None}\PY{p}{,} \PY{k+kc}{None}\PY{p}{,} \PY{n}{n\PYZus{}bands}\PY{p}{]}\PY{p}{)}
        \PY{n}{y}\PY{o}{.}\PY{n}{set\PYZus{}shape}\PY{p}{(}\PY{p}{[}\PY{k+kc}{None}\PY{p}{,} \PY{k+kc}{None}\PY{p}{,} \PY{l+m+mi}{6}\PY{p}{]}\PY{p}{)}
        \PY{k}{return} \PY{n}{X}\PY{p}{,} \PY{n}{y}

    \PY{n}{ds} \PY{o}{=} \PY{n}{tf}\PY{o}{.}\PY{n}{data}\PY{o}{.}\PY{n}{Dataset}\PY{o}{.}\PY{n}{from\PYZus{}tensor\PYZus{}slices}\PY{p}{(}\PY{n}{file\PYZus{}list}\PY{p}{)}
    \PY{k}{if} \PY{n}{augment\PYZus{}data}\PY{p}{:}
        \PY{n}{ds} \PY{o}{=} \PY{n}{ds}\PY{o}{.}\PY{n}{shuffle}\PY{p}{(}\PY{n+nb}{len}\PY{p}{(}\PY{n}{file\PYZus{}list}\PY{p}{)}\PY{p}{,} \PY{n}{seed}\PY{o}{=}\PY{n}{SEED}\PY{p}{)}
    
    \PY{c+c1}{\PYZsh{} Use multiple parallel calls to speed up loading}
    \PY{n}{ds} \PY{o}{=} \PY{n}{ds}\PY{o}{.}\PY{n}{map}\PY{p}{(}\PY{n}{tf\PYZus{}mapper}\PY{p}{,} \PY{n}{num\PYZus{}parallel\PYZus{}calls}\PY{o}{=}\PY{n}{tf}\PY{o}{.}\PY{n}{data}\PY{o}{.}\PY{n}{AUTOTUNE}\PY{p}{)}
    \PY{k}{return} \PY{n}{ds}\PY{o}{.}\PY{n}{batch}\PY{p}{(}\PY{n}{batch\PYZus{}size}\PY{p}{)}\PY{o}{.}\PY{n}{prefetch}\PY{p}{(}\PY{n}{tf}\PY{o}{.}\PY{n}{data}\PY{o}{.}\PY{n}{AUTOTUNE}\PY{p}{)}

\PY{c+c1}{\PYZsh{} Apply to datasets}
\PY{n+nb}{print}\PY{p}{(}\PY{l+s+s1}{\PYZsq{}}\PY{l+s+s1}{Building high\PYZhy{}performance datasets (Parallel I/O) ...}\PY{l+s+s1}{\PYZsq{}}\PY{p}{)}
\PY{n}{train\PYZus{}ds} \PY{o}{=} \PY{n}{make\PYZus{}dataset}\PY{p}{(}\PY{n}{train\PYZus{}files}\PY{p}{,} \PY{n}{augment\PYZus{}data}\PY{o}{=}\PY{k+kc}{True}\PY{p}{,}  \PY{n}{batch\PYZus{}size}\PY{o}{=}\PY{n}{BATCH\PYZus{}SIZE}\PY{p}{)}
\PY{n}{val\PYZus{}ds}   \PY{o}{=} \PY{n}{make\PYZus{}dataset}\PY{p}{(}\PY{n}{val\PYZus{}files}\PY{p}{,}   \PY{n}{augment\PYZus{}data}\PY{o}{=}\PY{k+kc}{False}\PY{p}{,} \PY{n}{batch\PYZus{}size}\PY{o}{=}\PY{n}{BATCH\PYZus{}SIZE}\PY{p}{)}
\PY{n}{test\PYZus{}ds}  \PY{o}{=} \PY{n}{make\PYZus{}dataset}\PY{p}{(}\PY{n}{test\PYZus{}files}\PY{p}{,}  \PY{n}{augment\PYZus{}data}\PY{o}{=}\PY{k+kc}{False}\PY{p}{,} \PY{n}{batch\PYZus{}size}\PY{o}{=}\PY{n}{BATCH\PYZus{}SIZE}\PY{p}{)}
\PY{n+nb}{print}\PY{p}{(}\PY{l+s+s1}{\PYZsq{}}\PY{l+s+s1}{  Ready (Parallel mapping enabled)}\PY{l+s+s1}{\PYZsq{}}\PY{p}{)}

    \subsubsection{2.3 --- Building the CNN
Architecture}\label{building-the-cnn-architecture}

\paragraph{\texorpdfstring{\texttt{keras.Input(shape=(None,\ None,\ 4))}}{keras.Input(shape=(None, None, 4))}}\label{keras.inputshapenone-none-4}

The model entry point. \texttt{None,\ None} means the model accepts
\textbf{any image size} --- flexible for different tile dimensions.

\paragraph{\texorpdfstring{\texttt{layers.Conv2D(32,\ 3,\ padding=\textquotesingle{}same\textquotesingle{})}}{layers.Conv2D(32, 3, padding=\textquotesingle same\textquotesingle)}}\label{layers.conv2d32-3-paddingsame}

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  \texttt{32} : number of filters (each learns to detect a different
  feature)
\item
  \texttt{3} : kernel size (3×3)
\item
  \texttt{padding=\textquotesingle{}same\textquotesingle{}} : zero-pad
  the border → output same width/height as input
\end{itemize}

\paragraph{Why 32 → 64 → 128 filters?}\label{why-32-64-128-filters}

Early layers learn simple features (edges, colours) → few filters
needed. Later layers combine these into complex patterns (building
rooftops, road textures) → more filters needed to represent the
increased variety.

\paragraph{\texorpdfstring{\texttt{activation=\textquotesingle{}relu\textquotesingle{}}
inside
Conv2D}{activation=\textquotesingle relu\textquotesingle{} inside Conv2D}}\label{activationrelu-inside-conv2d}

Convenience argument: applies ReLU immediately after the convolution ---
equivalent to writing a separate
\texttt{layers.Activation(\textquotesingle{}relu\textquotesingle{})}
line.

\begin{Verbatim}[commandchars=\\\{\}]
\PY{k}{def}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n+nf}{build\PYZus{}simple\PYZus{}cnn}\PY{p}{(}\PY{n}{input\PYZus{}channels}\PY{o}{=}\PY{l+m+mi}{4}\PY{p}{,} \PY{n}{num\PYZus{}classes}\PY{o}{=}\PY{l+m+mi}{6}\PY{p}{)}\PY{p}{:}
\PY{+w}{    }\PY{l+s+sd}{\PYZdq{}\PYZdq{}\PYZdq{}}
\PY{l+s+sd}{    Build a Fully Convolutional Network for semantic segmentation.}

\PY{l+s+sd}{    Architecture: 3 convolutional blocks + 1×1 output head.}
\PY{l+s+sd}{    No pooling → spatial resolution is preserved throughout.}

\PY{l+s+sd}{    Input  : (H, W, input\PYZus{}channels)}
\PY{l+s+sd}{    Output : (H, W, num\PYZus{}classes)  — softmax class probabilities per pixel}
\PY{l+s+sd}{    \PYZdq{}\PYZdq{}\PYZdq{}}
    \PY{n}{inp} \PY{o}{=} \PY{n}{keras}\PY{o}{.}\PY{n}{Input}\PY{p}{(}\PY{n}{shape}\PY{o}{=}\PY{p}{(}\PY{k+kc}{None}\PY{p}{,} \PY{k+kc}{None}\PY{p}{,} \PY{n}{input\PYZus{}channels}\PY{p}{)}\PY{p}{,} \PY{n}{name}\PY{o}{=}\PY{l+s+s1}{\PYZsq{}}\PY{l+s+s1}{input}\PY{l+s+s1}{\PYZsq{}}\PY{p}{)}

    \PY{c+c1}{\PYZsh{} ── Block 1: detect simple features (edges, colour gradients) ────}
    \PY{n}{x} \PY{o}{=} \PY{n}{layers}\PY{o}{.}\PY{n}{Conv2D}\PY{p}{(}\PY{l+m+mi}{32}\PY{p}{,} \PY{l+m+mi}{3}\PY{p}{,} \PY{n}{padding}\PY{o}{=}\PY{l+s+s1}{\PYZsq{}}\PY{l+s+s1}{same}\PY{l+s+s1}{\PYZsq{}}\PY{p}{,} \PY{n}{activation}\PY{o}{=}\PY{l+s+s1}{\PYZsq{}}\PY{l+s+s1}{relu}\PY{l+s+s1}{\PYZsq{}}\PY{p}{)}\PY{p}{(}\PY{n}{inp}\PY{p}{)}
    \PY{n}{x} \PY{o}{=} \PY{n}{layers}\PY{o}{.}\PY{n}{BatchNormalization}\PY{p}{(}\PY{p}{)}\PY{p}{(}\PY{n}{x}\PY{p}{)}
    \PY{n}{x} \PY{o}{=} \PY{n}{layers}\PY{o}{.}\PY{n}{Conv2D}\PY{p}{(}\PY{l+m+mi}{32}\PY{p}{,} \PY{l+m+mi}{3}\PY{p}{,} \PY{n}{padding}\PY{o}{=}\PY{l+s+s1}{\PYZsq{}}\PY{l+s+s1}{same}\PY{l+s+s1}{\PYZsq{}}\PY{p}{,} \PY{n}{activation}\PY{o}{=}\PY{l+s+s1}{\PYZsq{}}\PY{l+s+s1}{relu}\PY{l+s+s1}{\PYZsq{}}\PY{p}{)}\PY{p}{(}\PY{n}{x}\PY{p}{)}
    \PY{n}{x} \PY{o}{=} \PY{n}{layers}\PY{o}{.}\PY{n}{BatchNormalization}\PY{p}{(}\PY{p}{)}\PY{p}{(}\PY{n}{x}\PY{p}{)}

    \PY{c+c1}{\PYZsh{} ── Block 2: detect mid\PYZhy{}level features (textures, surface types) ─}
    \PY{n}{x} \PY{o}{=} \PY{n}{layers}\PY{o}{.}\PY{n}{Conv2D}\PY{p}{(}\PY{l+m+mi}{64}\PY{p}{,} \PY{l+m+mi}{3}\PY{p}{,} \PY{n}{padding}\PY{o}{=}\PY{l+s+s1}{\PYZsq{}}\PY{l+s+s1}{same}\PY{l+s+s1}{\PYZsq{}}\PY{p}{,} \PY{n}{activation}\PY{o}{=}\PY{l+s+s1}{\PYZsq{}}\PY{l+s+s1}{relu}\PY{l+s+s1}{\PYZsq{}}\PY{p}{)}\PY{p}{(}\PY{n}{x}\PY{p}{)}
    \PY{n}{x} \PY{o}{=} \PY{n}{layers}\PY{o}{.}\PY{n}{BatchNormalization}\PY{p}{(}\PY{p}{)}\PY{p}{(}\PY{n}{x}\PY{p}{)}
    \PY{n}{x} \PY{o}{=} \PY{n}{layers}\PY{o}{.}\PY{n}{Conv2D}\PY{p}{(}\PY{l+m+mi}{64}\PY{p}{,} \PY{l+m+mi}{3}\PY{p}{,} \PY{n}{padding}\PY{o}{=}\PY{l+s+s1}{\PYZsq{}}\PY{l+s+s1}{same}\PY{l+s+s1}{\PYZsq{}}\PY{p}{,} \PY{n}{activation}\PY{o}{=}\PY{l+s+s1}{\PYZsq{}}\PY{l+s+s1}{relu}\PY{l+s+s1}{\PYZsq{}}\PY{p}{)}\PY{p}{(}\PY{n}{x}\PY{p}{)}
    \PY{n}{x} \PY{o}{=} \PY{n}{layers}\PY{o}{.}\PY{n}{BatchNormalization}\PY{p}{(}\PY{p}{)}\PY{p}{(}\PY{n}{x}\PY{p}{)}

    \PY{c+c1}{\PYZsh{} ── Block 3: detect abstract features (building shapes, tree crowns) ─}
    \PY{n}{x} \PY{o}{=} \PY{n}{layers}\PY{o}{.}\PY{n}{Conv2D}\PY{p}{(}\PY{l+m+mi}{128}\PY{p}{,} \PY{l+m+mi}{3}\PY{p}{,} \PY{n}{padding}\PY{o}{=}\PY{l+s+s1}{\PYZsq{}}\PY{l+s+s1}{same}\PY{l+s+s1}{\PYZsq{}}\PY{p}{,} \PY{n}{activation}\PY{o}{=}\PY{l+s+s1}{\PYZsq{}}\PY{l+s+s1}{relu}\PY{l+s+s1}{\PYZsq{}}\PY{p}{)}\PY{p}{(}\PY{n}{x}\PY{p}{)}
    \PY{n}{x} \PY{o}{=} \PY{n}{layers}\PY{o}{.}\PY{n}{BatchNormalization}\PY{p}{(}\PY{p}{)}\PY{p}{(}\PY{n}{x}\PY{p}{)}
    \PY{n}{x} \PY{o}{=} \PY{n}{layers}\PY{o}{.}\PY{n}{Conv2D}\PY{p}{(}\PY{l+m+mi}{128}\PY{p}{,} \PY{l+m+mi}{3}\PY{p}{,} \PY{n}{padding}\PY{o}{=}\PY{l+s+s1}{\PYZsq{}}\PY{l+s+s1}{same}\PY{l+s+s1}{\PYZsq{}}\PY{p}{,} \PY{n}{activation}\PY{o}{=}\PY{l+s+s1}{\PYZsq{}}\PY{l+s+s1}{relu}\PY{l+s+s1}{\PYZsq{}}\PY{p}{)}\PY{p}{(}\PY{n}{x}\PY{p}{)}
    \PY{n}{x} \PY{o}{=} \PY{n}{layers}\PY{o}{.}\PY{n}{BatchNormalization}\PY{p}{(}\PY{p}{)}\PY{p}{(}\PY{n}{x}\PY{p}{)}

    \PY{c+c1}{\PYZsh{} ── Output head: 1×1 conv reduces 128 channels → num\PYZus{}classes ─────}
    \PY{n}{out} \PY{o}{=} \PY{n}{layers}\PY{o}{.}\PY{n}{Conv2D}\PY{p}{(}\PY{n}{num\PYZus{}classes}\PY{p}{,} \PY{l+m+mi}{1}\PY{p}{,} \PY{n}{padding}\PY{o}{=}\PY{l+s+s1}{\PYZsq{}}\PY{l+s+s1}{same}\PY{l+s+s1}{\PYZsq{}}\PY{p}{,}
                        \PY{n}{activation}\PY{o}{=}\PY{l+s+s1}{\PYZsq{}}\PY{l+s+s1}{softmax}\PY{l+s+s1}{\PYZsq{}}\PY{p}{,} \PY{n}{name}\PY{o}{=}\PY{l+s+s1}{\PYZsq{}}\PY{l+s+s1}{output}\PY{l+s+s1}{\PYZsq{}}\PY{p}{)}\PY{p}{(}\PY{n}{x}\PY{p}{)}

    \PY{k}{return} \PY{n}{keras}\PY{o}{.}\PY{n}{Model}\PY{p}{(}\PY{n}{inputs}\PY{o}{=}\PY{n}{inp}\PY{p}{,} \PY{n}{outputs}\PY{o}{=}\PY{n}{out}\PY{p}{,} \PY{n}{name}\PY{o}{=}\PY{l+s+s1}{\PYZsq{}}\PY{l+s+s1}{SimpleCNN}\PY{l+s+s1}{\PYZsq{}}\PY{p}{)}


\PY{n}{cnn} \PY{o}{=} \PY{n}{build\PYZus{}simple\PYZus{}cnn}\PY{p}{(}\PY{n}{input\PYZus{}channels}\PY{o}{=}\PY{l+m+mi}{4}\PY{p}{,} \PY{n}{num\PYZus{}classes}\PY{o}{=}\PY{n}{NUM\PYZus{}CLASSES}\PY{p}{)}

\PY{p}{)}

    \subsubsection{2.4 --- Training the Model}\label{training-the-model}

\paragraph{How the training loop works (one
epoch):}\label{how-the-training-loop-works-one-epoch}

\begin{verbatim}
For each batch in training data:
    1. Forward Pass  : data flows through all layers → prediction
    2. Loss Calc.    : loss = difference between prediction and ground truth
    3. Backward Pass : gradients of loss w.r.t. every weight (backpropagation)
    4. Weight Update : Adam moves each weight slightly in the direction that reduces loss
Repeat for all batches → one epoch complete.
Then evaluate on Validation set.
\end{verbatim}

\paragraph{ModelCheckpoint --- why do we need
it?}\label{modelcheckpoint-why-do-we-need-it}

The model at the \textbf{last epoch} is rarely the best. Overfitting
causes validation accuracy to peak early and decline later.
\texttt{ModelCheckpoint} saves a snapshot whenever
\texttt{val\_accuracy} improves --- we keep the best.

\paragraph{Learning Rate --- what it
means}\label{learning-rate-what-it-means}

Think of it as the \textbf{step size} when walking downhill toward a
valley (the loss minimum): - Too large → overshoot the valley, bouncing
around - Too small → painfully slow progress - \texttt{LR\ =\ 0.001} → a
good balanced default for Adam

    \begin{tcolorbox}[breakable, size=fbox, boxrule=1pt, pad at break*=1mm,colback=cellbackground, colframe=cellborder]
\prompt{In}{incolor}{16}{\boxspacing}
\begin{Verbatim}[commandchars=\\\{\}]
\PY{n}{best\PYZus{}cnn\PYZus{}path} \PY{o}{=} \PY{n}{os}\PY{o}{.}\PY{n}{path}\PY{o}{.}\PY{n}{join}\PY{p}{(}\PY{n}{OUTPUT\PYZus{}DIR}\PY{p}{,} \PY{l+s+s1}{\PYZsq{}}\PY{l+s+s1}{best\PYZus{}simple\PYZus{}model.keras}\PY{l+s+s1}{\PYZsq{}}\PY{p}{)}

\PY{n}{checkpoint\PYZus{}cnn} \PY{o}{=} \PY{n}{ModelCheckpoint}\PY{p}{(}
    \PY{n}{filepath}\PY{o}{=}\PY{n}{best\PYZus{}cnn\PYZus{}path}\PY{p}{,}
    \PY{n}{monitor}\PY{o}{=}\PY{l+s+s1}{\PYZsq{}}\PY{l+s+s1}{val\PYZus{}accuracy}\PY{l+s+s1}{\PYZsq{}}\PY{p}{,}    \PY{c+c1}{\PYZsh{} track validation accuracy}
    \PY{n}{save\PYZus{}best\PYZus{}only}\PY{o}{=}\PY{k+kc}{True}\PY{p}{,}       \PY{c+c1}{\PYZsh{} only save when it improves}
    \PY{n}{mode}\PY{o}{=}\PY{l+s+s1}{\PYZsq{}}\PY{l+s+s1}{max}\PY{l+s+s1}{\PYZsq{}}\PY{p}{,}                \PY{c+c1}{\PYZsh{} higher accuracy = better}
    \PY{n}{verbose}\PY{o}{=}\PY{l+m+mi}{0}
\PY{p}{)}

\PY{n+nb}{print}\PY{p}{(}\PY{l+s+sa}{f}\PY{l+s+s1}{\PYZsq{}}\PY{l+s+s1}{Training Simple CNN for }\PY{l+s+si}{\PYZob{}}\PY{n}{EPOCHS\PYZus{}CNN}\PY{l+s+si}{\PYZcb{}}\PY{l+s+s1}{ epochs ...}\PY{l+s+s1}{\PYZsq{}}\PY{p}{)}

\PY{n+nb}{print}\PY{p}{(}\PY{l+s+s1}{\PYZsq{}}\PY{l+s+s1}{=}\PY{l+s+s1}{\PYZsq{}} \PY{o}{*} \PY{l+m+mi}{50}\PY{p}{)}

\PY{p}{)}

    \subsubsection{2.5 --- Training Curve
Analysis}\label{training-curve-analysis}

\paragraph{How to read training
curves:}\label{how-to-read-training-curves}

\textbf{Healthy training (generalising well):}

\begin{verbatim}
Loss                     Accuracy
  ↓ Train                  Train  ↑
  ↓ Val    (close)         Val    ↑   (close)
\end{verbatim}

\textbf{Overfitting:}

\begin{verbatim}
Loss                     Accuracy
  ↓ Train                  Train  ↑↑↑  (very high)
  ↑ Val    (diverging)     Val    →     (plateaued / dropping)
\end{verbatim}

With a tiny dataset (one repeated tile), expect overfitting. This is
normal --- it disappears with the full dataset.

    \begin{tcolorbox}[breakable, size=fbox, boxrule=1pt, pad at break*=1mm,colback=cellbackground, colframe=cellborder]
\prompt{In}{incolor}{17}{\boxspacing}
\begin{Verbatim}[commandchars=\\\{\}]
\PY{n}{fig}\PY{p}{,} \PY{n}{axes} \PY{o}{=} \PY{n}{plt}\PY{o}{.}\PY{n}{subplots}\PY{p}{(}\PY{l+m+mi}{1}\PY{p}{,} \PY{l+m+mi}{2}\PY{p}{,} \PY{n}{figsize}\PY{o}{=}\PY{p}{(}\PY{l+m+mi}{14}\PY{p}{,} \PY{l+m+mi}{5}\PY{p}{)}\PY{p}{)}
\PY{n}{epochs\PYZus{}range} \PY{o}{=} \PY{n+nb}{range}\PY{p}{(}\PY{l+m+mi}{1}\PY{p}{,} \PY{n}{EPOCHS\PYZus{}CNN} \PY{o}{+} \PY{l+m+mi}{1}\PY{p}{)}

    \subsubsection{2.6 --- Final Evaluation on Test
Set}\label{final-evaluation-on-test-set}

\paragraph{Why is Test Set kept
separate?}\label{why-is-test-set-kept-separate}

We never used the Test Set for training \emph{or} for model selection.
Evaluating on Test Set gives us an \textbf{honest estimate} of how the
model will perform on data it has genuinely never seen.

\paragraph{\texorpdfstring{Load the \emph{best}
checkpoint}{Load the best checkpoint}}\label{load-the-best-checkpoint}

We load the saved checkpoint (best epoch), not the model at the last
epoch.
