    \subsubsection{✅ Step 2 Summary}\label{step-2-summary}

\textbf{Concepts mastered:} - Why convolution is far more efficient than
fully-connected layers for images - Role of BatchNormalization, ReLU,
and Softmax - How to recognise overfitting from training curves - Why
ModelCheckpoint saves the best --- not the last --- model

\textbf{Key observation:} Simple CNN overfits on small data. In Step 3
we'll use \textbf{U-Net} --- a more powerful architecture designed to
handle this better.

\textbf{→ Next: Step 3 --- U-Net with Skip Connections}

    \begin{center}\rule{0.5\linewidth}{0.5pt}\end{center}

\section{━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━}\label{section}

\section{Step 3: U-Net --- Encoder-Decoder with Skip
Connections}\label{step-3-u-net-encoder-decoder-with-skip-connections}

\section{━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━}\label{section-1}

\subsection{The problem with a plain
CNN}\label{the-problem-with-a-plain-cnn}

Our Step-2 CNN kept the image at its full resolution throughout. That's
fine, but it has a fundamental weakness:

\textbf{Fine spatial details get blended away by many conv layers.}

To detect the precise boundary between a building and asphalt we need
\emph{sharp spatial detail} --- but plain stacked convolutions gradually
smear it.

\subsection{The U-Net solution}\label{the-u-net-solution}

U-Net was invented in 2015 for biomedical image segmentation. Today it
is the \textbf{standard backbone} for aerial/satellite segmentation too.

\subsubsection{Core idea: Encoder--Decoder + Skip
Connections}\label{core-idea-encoderdecoder-skip-connections}

\begin{verbatim}
        [Input: full-resolution image]
               |
        ┌──── Encoder ────┐
        │ ↓ MaxPool       │   Compress: image halves, channels double
        │ ↓ MaxPool       │   Learns increasingly abstract features
        │ ↓ MaxPool       │
        └─── Bottleneck ──┘   Most compressed representation
               |
        ┌──── Decoder ────┐
        │ ↑ UpSample      │   Expand: image doubles, channels halve
        │ ↑ UpSample      │   Reconstructs spatial resolution
        │ ↑ UpSample      │
        └─────────────────┘
               |
        [Output: original-size class map]
\end{verbatim}

\subsubsection{Skip Connections --- the key
innovation}\label{skip-connections-the-key-innovation}

\begin{verbatim}
Encoder Level 1 ──────────────────────────────→ Decoder Level 1
    ↓                                           ↑  (concatenate)
Encoder Level 2 ──────────────────────────→ Decoder Level 2
    ↓                                       ↑
Encoder Level 3 ────────────────────→ Decoder Level 3
    ↓                               ↑
Encoder Level 4 ──────────→ Decoder Level 4
    ↓                       ↑
      Bottleneck ───────────→
\end{verbatim}

Each skip connection is a \textbf{direct bridge} from the encoder to the
decoder: - Encoder Level 1 captured sharp edges at full resolution -
That detail flows directly to Decoder Level 1 --- nothing is lost

\textbf{Why is that better?} Plain CNN: fine details (building edge)
gradually get blurred through deep layers. U-Net: those details are
\textbf{bypass-routed} straight to where reconstruction happens.

    \subsubsection{Key New Concepts in
U-Net}\label{key-new-concepts-in-u-net}

\paragraph{\texorpdfstring{MaxPooling
(\texttt{MaxPool2D(2)})}{MaxPooling (MaxPool2D(2))}}\label{maxpooling-maxpool2d2}

Halves the spatial dimensions, keeping the most prominent features:

\begin{verbatim}
Before MaxPool 2×2:    After MaxPool:
┌──┬──┬──┬──┐          ┌──┬──┐
│1 │3 │2 │4 │          │3 │4 │   ← maximum value in each 2×2 window
│5 │7 │6 │8 │  →  →    │7 │9 │
├──┼──┼──┼──┤
│2 │4 │9 │1 │
│6 │8 │3 │2 │
└──┴──┴──┴──┘
Output size is halved. The receptive field (visible area) is doubled.
\end{verbatim}

\paragraph{\texorpdfstring{UpSampling
(\texttt{UpSampling2D(2)})}{UpSampling (UpSampling2D(2))}}\label{upsampling-upsampling2d2}

Doubles spatial dimensions (inverse of MaxPool):

\begin{verbatim}
Before:          After (Nearest Neighbour):
┌──┬──┐          ┌──┬──┬──┬──┐
│3 │4 │  →  →    │3 │3 │4 │4 │   ← each pixel repeated 2× in each direction
│7 │9 │          │7 │7 │9 │9 │
└──┴──┘          └──┴──┴──┴──┘
\end{verbatim}

\paragraph{\texorpdfstring{Concatenate
(\texttt{layers.Concatenate})}{Concatenate (layers.Concatenate)}}\label{concatenate-layers.concatenate}

Merges UpSampling output with the skip feature map \textbf{along the
channel axis}:

\begin{verbatim}
From UpSampling:  (H, W, 256 channels)
From Skip:        (H, W, 256 channels)
After Concat:     (H, W, 512 channels)   ← channels are juxtaposed
\end{verbatim}

The decoder now has \emph{both} abstract context (from the bottleneck)
and fine spatial detail (from the skip) available at the same time.

    \begin{tcolorbox}[breakable, size=fbox, boxrule=1pt, pad at break*=1mm,colback=cellbackground, colframe=cellborder]
\prompt{In}{incolor}{19}{\boxspacing}
\begin{Verbatim}[commandchars=\\\{\}]
\PY{k+kn}{from}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n+nn}{tensorflow}\PY{n+nn}{.}\PY{n+nn}{keras}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{k+kn}{import} \PY{n}{layers}

\PY{k}{def}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n+nf}{conv\PYZus{}block}\PY{p}{(}\PY{n}{x}\PY{p}{,} \PY{n}{num\PYZus{}filters}\PY{p}{,} \PY{n}{block\PYZus{}name}\PY{p}{)}\PY{p}{:}
\PY{+w}{    }\PY{l+s+sd}{\PYZdq{}\PYZdq{}\PYZdq{}}
\PY{l+s+sd}{    Standard double\PYZhy{}convolution block used throughout U\PYZhy{}Net:}
\PY{l+s+sd}{    Conv2D → BN → ReLU → Conv2D → BN → ReLU}

\PY{l+s+sd}{    Args:}
\PY{l+s+sd}{        x           : input tensor}
\PY{l+s+sd}{        num\PYZus{}filters : number of convolutional filters}
\PY{l+s+sd}{        block\PYZus{}name  : string prefix for layer names (aids debugging)}
\PY{l+s+sd}{    Returns:}
\PY{l+s+sd}{        Output tensor with shape (H, W, num\PYZus{}filters)}
\PY{l+s+sd}{    \PYZdq{}\PYZdq{}\PYZdq{}}

    \PY{n}{x} \PY{o}{=} \PY{n}{layers}\PY{o}{.}\PY{n}{BatchNormalization}\PY{p}{(}\PY{n}{name}\PY{o}{=}\PY{l+s+sa}{f}\PY{l+s+s1}{\PYZsq{}}\PY{l+s+si}{\PYZob{}}\PY{n}{block\PYZus{}name}\PY{l+s+si}{\PYZcb{}}\PY{l+s+s1}{\PYZus{}bn1}\PY{l+s+s1}{\PYZsq{}}\PY{p}{)}\PY{p}{(}\PY{n}{x}\PY{p}{)}
    \PY{n}{x} \PY{o}{=} \PY{n}{layers}\PY{o}{.}\PY{n}{Conv2D}\PY{p}{(}\PY{n}{num\PYZus{}filters}\PY{p}{,} \PY{l+m+mi}{3}\PY{p}{,} \PY{n}{padding}\PY{o}{=}\PY{l+s+s1}{\PYZsq{}}\PY{l+s+s1}{same}\PY{l+s+s1}{\PYZsq{}}\PY{p}{,}

                      \PY{n}{name}\PY{o}{=}\PY{l+s+sa}{f}\PY{l+s+s1}{\PYZsq{}}\PY{l+s+si}{\PYZob{}}\PY{n}{block\PYZus{}name}\PY{l+s+si}{\PYZcb{}}\PY{l+s+s1}{\PYZus{}c2}\PY{l+s+s1}{\PYZsq{}}\PY{p}{)}\PY{p}{(}\PY{n}{x}\PY{p}{)}

\PY{k}{def}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n+nf}{build\PYZus{}unet}\PY{p}{(}\PY{n}{input\PYZus{}channels}\PY{o}{=}\PY{l+m+mi}{5}\PY{p}{,} \PY{n}{num\PYZus{}classes}\PY{o}{=}\PY{l+m+mi}{6}\PY{p}{,} \PY{n}{base\PYZus{}filters}\PY{o}{=}\PY{l+m+mi}{32}\PY{p}{)}\PY{p}{:}
\PY{+w}{    }\PY{l+s+sd}{\PYZdq{}\PYZdq{}\PYZdq{}}
\PY{l+s+sd}{    Full U\PYZhy{}Net architecture for semantic segmentation.}

\PY{l+s+sd}{    Args:}
\PY{l+s+sd}{        input\PYZus{}channels : number of input bands (default 5: RGB+IR+Elev)}
\PY{l+s+sd}{        num\PYZus{}classes    : number of output classes (default 6)}
\PY{l+s+sd}{        base\PYZus{}filters   : filter count at the first encoder block.}
\PY{l+s+sd}{                         Doubles at each deeper level: 32→64→128→256→512}
\PY{l+s+sd}{    Returns:}
\PY{l+s+sd}{        keras.Model}
\PY{l+s+sd}{    \PYZdq{}\PYZdq{}\PYZdq{}}
    \PY{n}{f}   \PY{o}{=} \PY{n}{base\PYZus{}filters}
    \PY{n}{inp} \PY{o}{=} \PY{n}{keras}\PY{o}{.}\PY{n}{Input}\PY{p}{(}\PY{n}{shape}\PY{o}{=}\PY{p}{(}\PY{k+kc}{None}\PY{p}{,} \PY{k+kc}{None}\PY{p}{,} \PY{n}{input\PYZus{}channels}\PY{p}{)}\PY{p}{,} \PY{n}{name}\PY{o}{=}\PY{l+s+s1}{\PYZsq{}}\PY{l+s+s1}{input}\PY{l+s+s1}{\PYZsq{}}\PY{p}{)}

    \PY{c+c1}{\PYZsh{} ── ENCODER (downsampling path) ───────────────────────────────────}
    \PY{c+c1}{\PYZsh{} Each level: conv\PYZus{}block → save skip → MaxPool (÷ 2)}

    \PY{n}{e1} \PY{o}{=} \PY{n}{conv\PYZus{}block}\PY{p}{(}\PY{n}{inp}\PY{p}{,} \PY{n}{f}\PY{p}{,}    \PY{l+s+s1}{\PYZsq{}}\PY{l+s+s1}{enc1}\PY{l+s+s1}{\PYZsq{}}\PY{p}{)}        \PY{c+c1}{\PYZsh{} (H,   W,   32)}
    \PY{n}{p1} \PY{o}{=} \PY{n}{layers}\PY{o}{.}\PY{n}{MaxPooling2D}\PY{p}{(}\PY{l+m+mi}{2}\PY{p}{,} \PY{n}{name}\PY{o}{=}\PY{l+s+s1}{\PYZsq{}}\PY{l+s+s1}{pool1}\PY{l+s+s1}{\PYZsq{}}\PY{p}{)}\PY{p}{(}\PY{n}{e1}\PY{p}{)}   \PY{c+c1}{\PYZsh{} (H/2, W/2, 32)}

    \PY{n}{e2} \PY{o}{=} \PY{n}{conv\PYZus{}block}\PY{p}{(}\PY{n}{p1}\PY{p}{,}  \PY{n}{f}\PY{o}{*}\PY{l+m+mi}{2}\PY{p}{,}  \PY{l+s+s1}{\PYZsq{}}\PY{l+s+s1}{enc2}\PY{l+s+s1}{\PYZsq{}}\PY{p}{)}        \PY{c+c1}{\PYZsh{} (H/2, W/2, 64)}
    \PY{n}{p2} \PY{o}{=} \PY{n}{layers}\PY{o}{.}\PY{n}{MaxPooling2D}\PY{p}{(}\PY{l+m+mi}{2}\PY{p}{,} \PY{n}{name}\PY{o}{=}\PY{l+s+s1}{\PYZsq{}}\PY{l+s+s1}{pool2}\PY{l+s+s1}{\PYZsq{}}\PY{p}{)}\PY{p}{(}\PY{n}{e2}\PY{p}{)}   \PY{c+c1}{\PYZsh{} (H/4, W/4, 64)}

    \PY{n}{e3} \PY{o}{=} \PY{n}{conv\PYZus{}block}\PY{p}{(}\PY{n}{p2}\PY{p}{,}  \PY{n}{f}\PY{o}{*}\PY{l+m+mi}{4}\PY{p}{,}  \PY{l+s+s1}{\PYZsq{}}\PY{l+s+s1}{enc3}\PY{l+s+s1}{\PYZsq{}}\PY{p}{)}        \PY{c+c1}{\PYZsh{} (H/4,  W/4,  128)}
    \PY{n}{p3} \PY{o}{=} \PY{n}{layers}\PY{o}{.}\PY{n}{MaxPooling2D}\PY{p}{(}\PY{l+m+mi}{2}\PY{p}{,} \PY{n}{name}\PY{o}{=}\PY{l+s+s1}{\PYZsq{}}\PY{l+s+s1}{pool3}\PY{l+s+s1}{\PYZsq{}}\PY{p}{)}\PY{p}{(}\PY{n}{e3}\PY{p}{)}   \PY{c+c1}{\PYZsh{} (H/8, W/8, 128)}

    \PY{n}{e4} \PY{o}{=} \PY{n}{conv\PYZus{}block}\PY{p}{(}\PY{n}{p3}\PY{p}{,}  \PY{n}{f}\PY{o}{*}\PY{l+m+mi}{8}\PY{p}{,}  \PY{l+s+s1}{\PYZsq{}}\PY{l+s+s1}{enc4}\PY{l+s+s1}{\PYZsq{}}\PY{p}{)}        \PY{c+c1}{\PYZsh{} (H/8,  W/8,  256)}
    \PY{n}{p4} \PY{o}{=} \PY{n}{layers}\PY{o}{.}\PY{n}{MaxPooling2D}\PY{p}{(}\PY{l+m+mi}{2}\PY{p}{,} \PY{n}{name}\PY{o}{=}\PY{l+s+s1}{\PYZsq{}}\PY{l+s+s1}{pool4}\PY{l+s+s1}{\PYZsq{}}\PY{p}{)}\PY{p}{(}\PY{n}{e4}\PY{p}{)}   \PY{c+c1}{\PYZsh{} (H/16,W/16,256)}

    \PY{c+c1}{\PYZsh{} ── BOTTLENECK (most abstract representation) ─────────────────────}
    \PY{n}{b}  \PY{o}{=} \PY{n}{conv\PYZus{}block}\PY{p}{(}\PY{n}{p4}\PY{p}{,} \PY{n}{f}\PY{o}{*}\PY{l+m+mi}{16}\PY{p}{,} \PY{l+s+s1}{\PYZsq{}}\PY{l+s+s1}{bottleneck}\PY{l+s+s1}{\PYZsq{}}\PY{p}{)}   \PY{c+c1}{\PYZsh{} (H/16, W/16, 512)}

    \PY{n}{u4} \PY{o}{=} \PY{n}{layers}\PY{o}{.}\PY{n}{Concatenate}\PY{p}{(}\PY{n}{name}\PY{o}{=}\PY{l+s+s1}{\PYZsq{}}\PY{l+s+s1}{cat4}\PY{l+s+s1}{\PYZsq{}}\PY{p}{)}\PY{p}{(}\PY{p}{[}\PY{n}{u4}\PY{p}{,} \PY{n}{e4}\PY{p}{]}\PY{p}{)}      \PY{c+c1}{\PYZsh{} + skip e4 → 768}

    \PY{n}{u3} \PY{o}{=} \PY{n}{layers}\PY{o}{.}\PY{n}{UpSampling2D}\PY{p}{(}\PY{l+m+mi}{2}\PY{p}{,} \PY{n}{name}\PY{o}{=}\PY{l+s+s1}{\PYZsq{}}\PY{l+s+s1}{up3}\PY{l+s+s1}{\PYZsq{}}\PY{p}{)}\PY{p}{(}\PY{n}{d4}\PY{p}{)}         \PY{c+c1}{\PYZsh{} (H/4,  W/4,  256)}
    \PY{n}{u3} \PY{o}{=} \PY{n}{layers}\PY{o}{.}\PY{n}{Concatenate}\PY{p}{(}\PY{n}{name}\PY{o}{=}\PY{l+s+s1}{\PYZsq{}}\PY{l+s+s1}{cat3}\PY{l+s+s1}{\PYZsq{}}\PY{p}{)}\PY{p}{(}\PY{p}{[}\PY{n}{u3}\PY{p}{,} \PY{n}{e3}\PY{p}{]}\PY{p}{)}      \PY{c+c1}{\PYZsh{} + skip e3}
    \PY{n}{d3} \PY{o}{=} \PY{n}{conv\PYZus{}block}\PY{p}{(}\PY{n}{u3}\PY{p}{,} \PY{n}{f}\PY{o}{*}\PY{l+m+mi}{4}\PY{p}{,}  \PY{l+s+s1}{\PYZsq{}}\PY{l+s+s1}{dec3}\PY{l+s+s1}{\PYZsq{}}\PY{p}{)}                   \PY{c+c1}{\PYZsh{} (H/4,  W/4,  128)}

    \PY{n}{u2} \PY{o}{=} \PY{n}{layers}\PY{o}{.}\PY{n}{UpSampling2D}\PY{p}{(}\PY{l+m+mi}{2}\PY{p}{,} \PY{n}{name}\PY{o}{=}\PY{l+s+s1}{\PYZsq{}}\PY{l+s+s1}{up2}\PY{l+s+s1}{\PYZsq{}}\PY{p}{)}\PY{p}{(}\PY{n}{d3}\PY{p}{)}         \PY{c+c1}{\PYZsh{} (H/2,  W/2,  128)}
    \PY{n}{u2} \PY{o}{=} \PY{n}{layers}\PY{o}{.}\PY{n}{Concatenate}\PY{p}{(}\PY{n}{name}\PY{o}{=}\PY{l+s+s1}{\PYZsq{}}\PY{l+s+s1}{cat2}\PY{l+s+s1}{\PYZsq{}}\PY{p}{)}\PY{p}{(}\PY{p}{[}\PY{n}{u2}\PY{p}{,} \PY{n}{e2}\PY{p}{]}\PY{p}{)}      \PY{c+c1}{\PYZsh{} + skip e2}

    \PY{n}{u1} \PY{o}{=} \PY{n}{layers}\PY{o}{.}\PY{n}{Concatenate}\PY{p}{(}\PY{n}{name}\PY{o}{=}\PY{l+s+s1}{\PYZsq{}}\PY{l+s+s1}{cat1}\PY{l+s+s1}{\PYZsq{}}\PY{p}{)}\PY{p}{(}\PY{p}{[}\PY{n}{u1}\PY{p}{,} \PY{n}{e1}\PY{p}{]}\PY{p}{)}      \PY{c+c1}{\PYZsh{} + skip e1}

    \subsubsection{3.2 --- Why a Lower Learning Rate for
U-Net?}\label{why-a-lower-learning-rate-for-u-net}

U-Net has \textasciitilde7.86 million parameters vs \textasciitilde215K
in the simple CNN. With more parameters, large gradient updates can
destabilise training.

A smaller LR (\texttt{1e-4} = 0.0001, ten times smaller than CNN's
\texttt{1e-3}) keeps updates conservative --- more stable but equally
effective over more epochs.

\subsubsection{3.3 --- Training U-Net with 5
Bands}\label{training-u-net-with-5-bands}

We now include the \textbf{Elevation} band as the 5th input channel. Why
elevation helps: - Buildings: tall → high elevation values - Cars: small
positive elevation - Asphalt: near-zero elevation

    \begin{tcolorbox}[breakable, size=fbox, boxrule=1pt, pad at break*=1mm,colback=cellbackground, colframe=cellborder]
\prompt{In}{incolor}{20}{\boxspacing}
\begin{Verbatim}[commandchars=\\\{\}]

    \subsubsection{3.4 --- Prediction
Visualisation}\label{prediction-visualisation}

\paragraph{\texorpdfstring{\texttt{np.argmax(pred\_probs,\ axis=-1)}
explained}{np.argmax(pred\_probs, axis=-1) explained}}\label{np.argmaxpred_probs-axis-1-explained}

U-Net outputs shape (H, W, 6): for every pixel, a probability
distribution over 6 classes. \texttt{argmax} selects the class with the
highest probability:

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\NormalTok{pred\_probs[row, col] }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ [}\FloatTok{0.05}\NormalTok{, }\FloatTok{0.01}\NormalTok{, }\FloatTok{0.02}\NormalTok{, }\FloatTok{0.10}\NormalTok{, }\FloatTok{0.80}\NormalTok{, }\FloatTok{0.02}\NormalTok{]}
\NormalTok{np.argmax(...)        → }\DecValTok{4}\NormalTok{    (Car — highest probability)}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

Result shape: (H, W) --- one integer class ID per pixel.

\paragraph{What should good predictions look
like?}\label{what-should-good-predictions-look-like}

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  Prediction should \textbf{broadly match} the ground-truth label map
\item
  Edges may be slightly blurry (this improves with full dataset)
\item
  U-Net typically produces \textbf{sharper boundaries} than a plain CNN
\end{itemize}

Conclusions}\label{final-comparison-and-conclusions}

Metric & Simple CNN & U-Net \\

\bottomrule\noalign{}

\subsection{Why U-Net wins on a full
dataset}\label{why-u-net-wins-on-a-full-dataset}

With one repeated tile both models overfit similarly. On the full
Potsdam dataset (thousands of diverse tiles): - Skip connections allow
sharp boundary recovery - U-Net achieves significantly higher IoU and
pixel accuracy - Expected performance with full data:
\textbf{\textgreater80\% accuracy} for both models, \textbf{U-Net
clearly better}

\# 🎓 Complete Glossary \\
Term \textbar{} Definition \textbar{} ---\textbar---\textbar{}
\textbf{Semantic Segmentation} \textbar{} Assigning a class label to
every pixel in an image \textbar{} \textbf{GeoTIFF} \textbar{}
Multi-band image format with embedded geographic metadata \textbar{}
\textbf{Band} \textbar{} A single-channel layer in a multi-spectral
image \textbar{} \textbf{Normalisation} \textbar{} Rescaling values to a
fixed range (e.g.~{[}0, 1{]}) \textbar{} \textbf{One-Hot Encoding}
\textbar{} Representing class integer as a binary vector \textbar{}
\textbf{Convolution} \textbar{} Sliding a small filter over an image to
detect spatial patterns \textbar{} \textbf{Kernel / Filter} \textbar{}
The small weight matrix used in convolution (e.g.~3×3) \textbar{}
\textbf{Feature Map} \textbar{} The output of a convolutional layer ---
a learned representation \textbar{} \textbf{BatchNormalization}
\textbar{} Standardising layer activations for stable training
\textbar{} \textbf{ReLU} \textbar{} Activation: \texttt{max(0,\ x)} ---
introduces non-linearity \textbar{} \textbf{Softmax} \textbar{} Converts
raw scores into probabilities summing to 1 \textbar{} \textbf{K-Fold CV}
\textbar{} Splitting data into K equal folds for unbiased evaluation
\textbar{} \textbf{Overfitting} \textbar{} Model memorises training
data; fails to generalise \textbar{} \textbf{Data Augmentation}
\textbar{} Creating diverse variants of training data geometrically
\textbar{} \textbf{Learning Rate} \textbar{} Step size for the optimiser
during weight updates \textbar{} \textbf{Adam} \textbar{} Adaptive
gradient optimiser --- default choice for deep learning \textbar{}
\textbf{Categorical CE} \textbar{} Cross-entropy loss for multi-class
classification \textbar{} \textbf{ModelCheckpoint} \textbar{} Keras
callback that saves the best model during training \textbar{}
\textbf{MaxPooling} \textbar{} Halves spatial dimensions by keeping the
maximum per window \textbar{} \textbf{UpSampling} \textbar{} Doubles
spatial dimensions by repeating pixels \textbar{} \textbf{Skip
Connection} \textbar{} Direct pathway from encoder level to
corresponding decoder level \textbar{} \textbf{Encoder} \textbar{}
Downsampling path that extracts increasingly abstract features
\textbar{} \textbf{Decoder} \textbar{} Upsampling path that reconstructs
full-resolution class map \textbar{} \textbf{Bottleneck} \textbar{} The
most compressed layer --- global image context \textbar{}
\textbf{Argmax} \textbar{} Selects the index of the maximum value;
converts probabilities → class \textbar{} \\
\end{longtable}

Dataset}\label{next-steps-with-the-full-dataset}

  Expect both models to exceed \textbf{80\% test accuracy}
\item
  U-Net will produce visibly sharper segmentation boundaries
\end{enumerate}
